# Reconstruction of the critical line theorem

## Starting point

The aim is to prove for ζ that all its non-trivial zeros have real part 1/2. The comparison of filters in the README uses precisely this input. Using it to prove the input would be circular.

In main.tex of paper 46, the proposition prop:growing-window reduces the aim to an upper bound for G on intervals [Y,Y^J], with J>1 fixed and one and the same Cθ on arbitrarily large windows for each θ>1/2. The proposition prop:simultaneous-slope deduces it from the control of a mean of columns over the same arithmetic data. In annex.tex, i4:thm-final assembles the hypotheses on loss, divisors, counts and Selmer, together with the results listed in i4:rem-trust. Alpoge's note (https://alpo.ge/remark.pdf, pp. 3–4) describes a multiplicity obtained by theta lifts and a balance between norms and divisibility. These dependencies are background; that balance is not adopted here as an axiom.

## Writing guide according to the annex of 46

The classification of statements is in annex.tex, lines 128–149. The inventories of results whose proof is referred to the formalisation begin at lines 5776, 10025, 16871, 23185 and 25197 (final referral at 31940). For a mixed proposition only the indicated item is taken. Within each scheme (L) identifies the literature background, (N) the new content in the form needed and (R) the routine operation. The order of writing is determined by following those items from the families theorem i4:thm-final.

For the present work, the item is **i4:A-prop-open (O3)**, stated at line 28763 and broken down in 31867–31885. It has two obligations: to obtain MI*₂ in the realisation and to deduce the vanishings of the definite and division pairings. The written proof of c5 (26212–26229) already explains how to use the multiplicity identity at the base tuple. Of the first obligation, the section «A single archimedean vector» simplifies the descent of rows; the scheme of (O3) also invokes the Siegel–Weil diagonal and the realisation at the witness points. For the second obligation, the vanishing on the definite base and the column formula in the quotient under the boundary law are given below. The diagonal estimate is also reconstructed from four bounds, which have to be verified in the realisation. The reduction of ranks does not by itself certify (O3).

## What precision a construction by averages needs

Let Y be a random variable of finite support in [1,∞), with mean x. For every n≥2 let b_n(t)=(t−n)_+. By the definition of the G of the paper one obtains the exact identity

    E G(Y) − G(x) = ∑_{n≥2} Λ(n)/n [E b_n(Y)−b_n(x)] − E[Y log(Y/x)].

The sum is finite. The linear terms have disappeared because E Y=x. Each bracket is non-negative by convexity. By log v≤v−1,

    0 ≤ E[Y log(Y/x)] ≤ E[Y(Y/x−1)] = Var(Y)/x,

where the first inequality follows from the convexity of t log t. Therefore

    G(x) ≤ E G(Y) + Var(Y)/x.

This allows a biased choice, even with distant atoms: it suffices to preserve the barycentre exactly and to control the second moment, without bounding the diameter. If at each x of the windows one had Var(Y)≤D x^(3/2) and E G(Y)≤Cθ x^θ, with Cθ,D uniform on them, one would obtain the criterion of the paper. The error allowed in standard deviation is of order x^(3/4). The manuscript already contains the case of interpolation by two endpoints and a two-sided comparison for samples at distance √x. The inequality above generalises the one-sided comparison; it does not establish the arithmetic mean.

There is moreover an immediate test against a misleading simplification. For any family of these laws with the indicated variance one has E G(Y)≥G(x)−D√x. No choice of weights, not even an adaptive one, can by itself suppress a positive excursion of order x^β with β>1/2. For a family of characters, a divisibility identity or another construction to be of use, it must supply an independent upper estimate for the real mean. This is the point to be compared with the multiplicity and the arithmetic cost described by Alpoge. That estimate has not been reproduced, nor has the target theorem been proved.

## A test with integer multiplicity and its exact residue

Let x≥4 be real, m=ceil(√x), X=mx, ψ(t)=∑_{q≤t}Λ(q) and H_m=∑_{j=1}^m 1/j. Consider the mean with multiplicity m

    D_m(x) = (1/m) ∑_{j=1}^m [X/j − ψ(X/j)].

Unconditionally G(x)=D_m(x)+O(√x), uniformly in x. Indeed, counting the pairs jq≤X with j≤m gives

    ∑_{j≤m} ψ(X/j) = mψ(x) + ∑_{x<q≤X} Λ(q) floor(X/q).

By the factorisation of floor(X)!, the last sum equals log(floor(X)!) minus the sum corresponding to q≤x. If E=∑_{q≤x}Λ(q){X/q}, one gets exactly

    G(x)−D_m(x) = log(floor(X)!)/m − x log x + (1+γ_E)x − xH_m + E/m.

Stirling gives log(floor(X)!)=X log X−X+O(log(2X)), even for real X. Moreover 0≤E≤ψ(x)=O(x) by Chebyshev and H_m=log m+γ_E+O(1/m). Substituting gives the claim. This deduction uses the factorial identity already recorded in the index of the corpus, and keeps the sign of the rounding error in the exact formula.

One can even replace ψ by θ(t)=∑_{p≤t}log p at cost O(√x). Indeed ψ(t)−θ(t)=O(√t) by Chebyshev applied to each power: the quadratic term costs O(√t) and the others O(t^(1/3)log(2t)). On averaging, ∑_{j≤m}√(X/j)/m≤2√x. Thus it suffices to prove, for each ε>0 and on the growing windows of the manuscript, the one-sided inequality

    ∑_{j=1}^m θ(mx/j) ≥ mx H_m − C_ε m x^(1/2+ε).

With it the proposition prop:growing-window and the functional symmetry would give RH. The constant must be common to the windows. A direct application of the prime number theorem gives only a loss o(x H_m) after dividing by m (uniformity because all the arguments are ≥x), which is insufficient. The multiplicity √x already eliminates the rounding errors to the desired precision; it remains to control the prime part of the mean. One obtains a target for an independent estimate, and the pending inequality is not attributed to the factorisation of a factorial.

## What the mean sees at a single frequency of the explicit formula

Define A_m f(x)=m⁻¹∑_{j≤m}f(mx/j), with the same multiplicities as in D_m. For s=β+it, 0<β<1, its action on f(x)=x^s is multiplication by L_m(s)=m^{s−1}∑_{j≤m}j^(−s). One has the expansion with explicit error

    L_m(s) = 1/(1−s) + m^{s−1}ζ(s) + 1/(2m) + r_m(s),
    |r_m(s)| ≤ |s(s+1)| / [8(1+β)m²].

Proof: for β>1, apply the trapezoidal rule to t^(−s) from m to infinity on intervals of length one. The error per interval is bounded by one eighth of the integral of the modulus of the second derivative, since the kernel of the error is (t−j)(j+1−t)/2. Subtracting the tail of ζ gives the formula as written. The integral representation of the remainder before multiplying by m^{s−1} converges locally uniformly already in β>−1. Analytic continuation gives the identity in the strip and the integration of |s(s+1)|t^(−β−2) gives the bound.

In particular, for every fixed zero ρ=β+iγ in the strip, the correction m^{ρ−1}ζ(ρ) vanishes exactly, but 1/(1−ρ) survives. In the one-term model of the prime deficit x^ρ/ρ, one therefore obtains x^ρ/[ρ(1−ρ)] plus an error O_ρ(x^β/m). For m≥√x and β<1 that error is o_ρ(√x). The approximation is uniform in all those m, and by linearity also in any convex mixture of them, even with weights depending on x. The proof treats a fixed frequency and does not exchange this expansion with the infinite sum over zeros.

**Decision on the mechanism.** Raising the factorial multiplicity or mixing positive multiplicities in this range leaves intact, to leading order, the complex coefficient of each possible exterior zero. The sign of the real term changes with the phase; the computation does not establish an excursion of the whole sum. In the proposition prop:pointwise of paper 46, the different ingredient is an upper comparison for the gain g by means of slopes (P8), together with the lower control g≥P_*−LoP (P7). The factorial reproduces the discretisation precision, but does not supply that pair of inequalities on the same object. We do not pursue a spectral gain based exclusively on readjusting m.

## A determinant that does give size and divisibility on the same integer

For an integer n≥2 take the evaluation determinant of 1,t,…,t^(n−1) at 0,…,n−1. Its absolute value V_n is ∏_{j=1}^{n−1}j!. For a prime power q=p^a, the number of difference factors divisible by q is

    C_q=∑_{k≥1}(n−kq)_+ = (n²/q − n + q {n/q}(1−{n/q}))/2      (q≤n).

The identity comes from writing n=kq+r. It also has a simultaneous optimality property: for any n distinct integer nodes the number of pairs congruent modulo q is the sum of binom(b_i,2) over the occupations of the classes. Moving a point from one class to another with two fewer points decreases that sum. Consecutive integers already balance the occupations for every q. Therefore V_n divides any Vandermonde determinant on n distinct integer nodes. This property concerns the determinant without weights.

Define w(t)=t {1/t}(1−{1/t}) for 0<t≤1. Summing C_q log p over all prime powers gives exactly

    G(n) = (2/n) log V_n − n log n + (1+γ_E)n − ∑_{q≤n} Λ(q)w(q/n).

The size of V_n is computed separately without primes. Stirling in log(j!) gives log V_n = (n²/2)log n−3n²/4+O(n log n): it suffices to compare ∑j log j with its integral, with error O(n log n), and to add the linear terms. Consequently elementary divisibility gives the upper bound G(n)≤(γ_E−1/2)n+O(log n). Its distance from the required scale is linear. The missing constant is positive (γ_E−1/2≈0.0772157), and has a precise interpretation: it is the mean mass of the occupation remainders. Indeed, for Re s>1, the pair identity, now as an identity in t, gives by term-by-term integration

    ∫₀¹ w(t)t^(s−1)dt = 2ζ(s)/[s(s+1)] − 1/(s−1) + 1/s.

It suffices to use w(t)=2∑_{k≥1}(1−kt)_+−1/t+1. The bound w(t)≤t/4 allows continuation to Re s>−1 (the removable singularities are taken by limit). At s=1 the value is γ_E−1/2. By PNT and uniform approximation of the continuous function w on [0,1], with w(0)=0, the sum of remainders equals (γ_E−1/2)n+o(n). This recovers the main term, without the final precision.

**Reduction of the sum to be estimated.** The q≤n^(3/4) cost altogether O(√n) by w(q/n)≤q/(4n) and Chebyshev. The non-prime powers up to n also cost O(√n), using w≤1/4 and ψ(n)−θ(n)=O(√n). Thus, at integers, a target equivalent to precision O(√n) is

    G(n) = (γ_E−1/2)n − ∑_{n^(3/4)<p≤n} (log p) w(p/n) + O(√n).

Only a one-sided lower bound for this sum on the windows of the paper is needed. No term of the sum is negative. The weight is small when n/p is close to an integer, so that counting primes without controlling their distribution within those intervals loses information. At a zero ζ(ρ)=0, its Mellin transform equals 1/[ρ(1−ρ)], which is non-zero. In particular, the determinant does not by itself eliminate the spectral obstruction; it quantifies exactly what part of the divisibility would have to be justified to reach the sign. Changing the nodes while keeping only the universal divisibility V_n does not increase it.

## Cost of approximating the remainder by factorial minorants

An elementary factorial profile on (0,1] is floor(a/t), with 0<a≤1: its sum against Λ(q) at t=q/n is log(floor(an)!). To allow also continuous averages and coefficients of both signs, let ν be a finite real signed measure on (0,1] with ∫a dν(a)=0. Put h(t)=∫floor(a/t)dν(a). The balance condition gives |h|≤||ν||. For Re s>1, by absolute integration,

    ∫₀¹ h(t)t^(s−1)dt = ζ(s) ∫a^s dν(a) / s.

The balance eliminates the pole at s=1 and both sides continue holomorphically to Re s>0. In particular this whole class of approximants annihilates the first known zero ρ=1/2+iγ, 14.13<γ<14.14 (datum Z1 of paper 46).

Suppose h≤w almost everywhere, E=w−h, δ=∫₀¹E. The transform of w computed above forces

    1/(1/4+γ²) ≤ ∫₀¹ E(t)/√t dt.                                      (*)

If h≥0, one has moreover E≤t/4. Among the functions with that bound and mass δ, the maximum of the right-hand side is attained by putting E=t/4 up to b=√(8δ) and zero afterwards. Indeed, on subtracting that optimal profile, the product of the difference by t^(−1/2)−b^(−1/2) is non-positive on both intervals, and the integral of the difference is zero. The maximum equals b^(3/2)/6. From (*) it follows that

    δ ≥ [6/(1/4+γ²)]^(4/3)/8 > 1/1000.

Thus, even a continuous superposition of balanced factorial minorants lying between zero and w loses at least n/1000 in the main mass at scale n. It is a statement about the integral of the profile; it does not assume a uniform PNT for variable profiles.

If we admit negative h, we write M=||E||∞. By the same rearrangement argument, now with the profile M on [0,δ/M], the integral in (*) is ≤2√(Mδ). Therefore

    ||(−h)_+||∞ ≥ 1/[4δ(1/4+γ²)²] − 1/4.

To achieve δ≤C/√n, the negative depth must grow at least like c√n/C−1/4, even allowing all scales. The argument does not prevent that depth from being confined to t<1/n, where no prime power of the sum is evaluated. In fact, such a concentration is a point that a construction with signed coefficients would have to exploit or compensate. To assess its usefulness one would still need to control the sum of the Stirling losses weighted by ν. Background on method: factorial minorants of phase 212 and transforms of Nyman type from the index; here the explicit value of the transform of the Vandermonde remainder fixes the loss.

## Exact construction above the cut and unavoidable cost of its variation

The freedom below 1/n allows the interpolation part to be solved exactly. Let F(t)=ν([t,1]). For t>0 one has h(t)=∑_{k≤1/t}F(kt). If h=w is prescribed on [1/n,1], finite Möbius inversion forces on that whole interval

    F(t)=∑_{k≤1/t} μ(k)w(kt)
        =2(1−t)−∑_{k≤1/t} μ(k)(1/(kt)−1).

For the second equality use the pair identity for w and exchange sums; ∑_{k|j}μ(k) annihilates all indices except j=1. The new terms enter with value zero at each cut point, so F is continuous and absolutely continuous on this interval, and F(1)=0. The measure of density −F'(a) on (1/n,1) realises the interpolation even at the endpoints. If its first moment is b, adding the atom of mass −2nb at 1/(2n) gives balance without modifying h on [1/n,1]. In particular the construction reproduces the exact value at each relevant q/n. No sign has been imposed below the cut.

The variation in the part already fixed is computed without carrying out the inversion numerically. With A(y)=∑_{k≤y}μ(k)/k, one has F'(t)=−2+t^(−2)A(1/t) almost everywhere. Therefore,

    ||ν|| on (1/n,1) = ∫₁ⁿ |A(y)−2/y²| dy.

No modification hidden below the cut affects that quantity. If one wished to control this cost by absolute values at scale O_ε(n^(1/2+ε)) for every n and every ε>0, the estimate would already imply RH. Indeed, it would give the identical bound for ∫₁ⁿ |A(y)|dy up to an additive error ≤2. By dyadic summation, ∫₁^∞ A(y)y^(−s)dy would be absolutely convergent and holomorphic in Re s>1/2. For Re s>1, by the absolutely convergent Möbius series, that integral is 1/[(s−1)ζ(s)]. The continuation excludes zeros to the right of the critical line. The implication requires the control at all cuts; it is not claimed for an arbitrary subsequence.

Thus the visible profile is realised, but there are two precautions in estimating it: the continuous mass of the balanced profile below the cut remains uncontrolled, and bounding the visible part by total variation already requires an estimate of strength comparable to the aim. A computation with Stirling that keeps the sign of the remainder, or an interpolation that prescribes only the discrete values at the primes, is not excluded by this last implication.

## Exact main term and localisation of the Stirling error

The choice of correcting atoms can be avoided altogether: we use the density of the inversion at all scales and subtract the continuous term before estimating. Define B(y)=2/y²−A(y) for y≥1 and

    R(v)=log(floor(v)!)−v log v+v,       0!=1.

By the change y=1/a in the density −F'(a), the factorisation gives, without error,

    S(n):=∑_{q≤n}Λ(q)w(q/n) = ∫₁ⁿ B(y)log(floor(n/y)!)dy.

Outside [1,n] the factorial equals one. The two integrals required by the continuous term are fixed. Indeed, the classical PNT with zero-free region gives ∑_{k≤y}μ(k)=O(y exp(−c√log y)); by partial summation and ∑μ(k)/k=0, A(y)=O(exp(−c'√log y)) for some c'>0. We use this classical unconditional estimate here only for the absolute convergence of the two logarithmic moments. The Mellin identity for A of the previous section, on passing to s=1 and differentiating from the right, gives

    ∫₁^∞ A(y)dy/y = 1,     ∫₁^∞ A(y)log y dy/y = γ_E.

One uses 1/[(s−1)ζ(s)]=1−γ_E(s−1)+O((s−1)²). The corresponding integrals for 2/y² are 1 and 1/2. Therefore ∫ B(y)[(n/y)log(n/y)−n/y]dy=(γ_E−1/2)n. We have obtained the signed identity

    S(n)−(γ_E−1/2)n = ∫₁^∞ B(y)R(n/y)dy.

By Stirling and the mean value theorem on replacing v by floor(v), |R(v)|≪log(2v) for v≥1; for 0<v<1 one has R(v)=v(1−log v)≤1. The part 2/y² of the integral costs O(log(2n)). Moreover |A(y)|≤1+1/y, since ∑_{k≤y}μ(k)floor(y/k)=1. Thus the stretch 1≤y≤√n costs O(√n log(2n)), even on taking absolute values. Combining with the determinant formula there remains

    G(n) = ∫_{√n}^∞ A(y) R(n/y) dy + O(√n log(2n)).

This time the linear term has been computed, and the whole portion where the argument of the factorial exceeds √n is controlled unconditionally to the precision x^(1/2+ε). The tail y>n also counts: there the integrand is exactly A(y)(n/y)(1+log(y/n)). A one-sided bound for the remaining integral with a constant uniform on the windows is still necessary. The absolute convergence used to evaluate the moments does not, by itself, provide that precision.

## Finite cut of the remainder without requiring information at all scales

Put L(v)=log(floor(v)!) and K(v)=L(4v)−4L(2v)+4L(v). The combination annihilates v and v log v identically, hence K(v)=R(4v)−4R(2v)+4R(v). In particular K(v)=0 for v<1/4, even if A(y) has no cancellation in the tail. For integers n≥4 it follows from the previous identity (reincorporating the small stretch before combining)

    G(4n)−4G(2n)+4G(n) = ∫_{√n}^{4n} A(y) K(n/y)dy + O(√n log(2n)).

Indeed, for y≤√n the three remainders are O(log(2n)), and |A(y)|≤2. The excluded terms 2/y² cost O(log(2n)). The sign of K still matters: K(1)=log(24/16)>0 and K(3/2)=log(720/1296)<0.

**Why growing windows suffice also for this finite integral.** Let H(x)=G(4x)−4G(2x)+4G(x). Its Mellin transform over [1,∞) is

    (2^s−2)² P(s) + E(s),

where P is that of paper 46 and E is entire: on changing variable only integrals over [1,2] and [1,4] are removed. Every exterior zero ρ with 1/2<Re ρ<1 keeps its simple pole because |2^ρ|<2. No real poles are introduced in Re s>0. The proof by Laplace derivatives of prop:growing-window in paper 46 applies literally to H: it is real, H(e^u)=O(exp(2u)), and it is precisely those properties and the simple poles off the real axis that it uses. Hence, for each θ>1/2 (θ<1 suffices), an upper bound Cθ n^θ for the finite integral at all the integers of arbitrarily large windows [Y,Y^J], with J>1 fixed, would suffice. To pass to real x, G has bounded derivative by the Chebyshev and factorial argument of the paper, hence H is Lipschitz; the windows can be contracted to any intermediate exponent 1<J'<J.

There is an alternative check under the stronger requirement of a bound H(n)≤C n^θ at all large integers. For fixed large n let u_k=G(2^k n)/2^k, a sequence that tends to zero by G(x)=o(x) (PNT, or the formula for S(n)). Then Δ²u_k=H(2^k n)/(4·2^k)≤Cn^θ r^k/4 with r=2^(θ−1)<1. The sequence v_k=Cn^θ r^k/[4(1−r)²] has exactly that second difference and tends to zero. Thus u_k−v_k is concave and tends to zero: none of its first differences can be negative because the following ones would be equal or smaller. Therefore u_0≤v_0. This verifies directly that the one-sided direction survives the recovery. The argument by poles allows separated windows to be used without having the bound at all the dyadic successors.

The second difference as an operation has antecedents in phase 213. What is obtained here is a finite arithmetic target on √n≤y≤4n with explicit kernel, keeping the sufficiency of separated windows. The bound for that pairing remains pending.

## A joint slope inequality that would suffice

Fix a host with the finite law of translations of the criterion, and write E for that law; the average of columns within each translation keeps its original rank weights. The proof of prop:pointwise of paper 46, before using P8, has these two inequalities:

    P_* ≤ g + LoP,     g ≤ s − μ∨ + M_* + K₀ + [ℓ].

The second comes from the lemma lem:s-lower and the identity K₅=M_*+K₀. Therefore the only combination of slopes that still needs an upper bound in this step is E(s−μ∨). Put D=s−μ∨−log|I|. If a joint comparison E D≤b_frame is realised, it suffices to verify in the same law

    E(LoP+K₀+[ℓ]+log|I|) + b_frame ≤ C u^K Z.

This gives directly the hypothesis of prop:simultaneous-slope. Unlike requiring P8 in each translation, D is allowed to be large in one as long as another compensates for it. The conclusion still requires the hosts, the previous identifications and their uniform constants.

A precise way of proving the joint comparison would be to represent the slopes by Euclidean lattices (or their determinants after restriction of scalars, with the normalisations verified). For rational probabilities p_i, choose positive integers k_i so that k_i r_i/R=p_i, R=∑ k_i r_i, where r_i is the rank of both lattices B_i,C_i compared in the fibre i; we omit atoms of mass zero. Suppose that μ(B_i)−μ(C_i)=D_i, with μ=−log(covolume)/rank. Form the orthogonal sums B,C with k_i copies of each pair. For **any** integral map T:B→C of full rank one has exactly

    E D = (log |det_R T| − log [C:T(B)])/R,

since the volume of T(B) is both |det_R T| vol(B) and [C:T(B)] vol(C). The real determinant is measured in orthonormal frames. Thus an archimedean ceiling and a divisibility floor on a single mixed determinant would suffice. Blocks connecting different translations are allowed. The multiplicity k_i incorporates the rank, without biasing the given law.

There is a condition that cannot be eliminated in this version: choosing an arbitrary integer matrix, or changing T to cT to gain divisibility, does not improve the balance. The index and the determinant both grow by |c|^R and their logarithmic difference is invariant. For the construction to be of use one has to exhibit lattices with the required D_i and estimate the two terms by independent arguments, with a normalised saving uniform in the host. We have not constructed such lattices nor that map. The formula makes precise what a lower-rank version of the slope step would have to realise; by itself it contributes no bound for E D.

## How to manufacture a square map from the rectangular maps

The frame theorem of 46 gives B_i:V_i→W that take N_i to CC. In general the dimensions r_i and κ are different; their individual determinants do not provide the proposed comparison. For a family over one and the same field, with r_i,κ positive, let S=∑r_i, d=gcd(κ,S), q=κ/d and k=S/d. Take q copies of each source and k copies of the target. The rank of both sides is qS=kκ. On the copy b of V_i consider the map v↦(c_{aib}B_i v)_{a=1}^k, and add these maps. One obtains a square matrix T(c) whose determinant is a polynomial in the scalars c_{aib}. Its not vanishing identically is a concrete algebraic requirement.

If that polynomial is non-zero, there exists an integral specialisation with each c_{aib} between 0 and r_i and det T≠0. Indeed, only the r_i columns of the block (i,b) contain that variable, and the determinant has degree at most r_i in it. Apply univariate interpolation on the indicated grid by induction. Thus the specialised map preserves the lattices. In real Euclidean spaces with orthogonal sum in the target, the volume of the images of an orthonormal basis of that block is ≤(√k r_i ||B_i||op)^{r_i}. Hadamard's inequality for the volume of all the blocks gives, in the convention of Euclidean slope −log(covolume)/rank,

    ∑_i (r_i/S)(μ(N_i)−log||B_i||op) − μ(CC)
        ≤ (1/2)log k + ∑_i (r_i/S)log r_i.

Here it has been used that the index of the integral image is at least one. The estimate is for real Euclidean lattices; the Hermitian convention of the paper, its heights by place and the factors of restriction of scalars require their own conversion. If some B_i has a kernel, the construction with complete sources is singular.

For any family of subspaces U_i⊂V_i there is a necessary test before doing interpolation: if T(c) is injective then

    ∑_i dim U_i ≤ (S/κ) dim(∑_i B_i U_i),

since the q copies of those subspaces are sent to k copies of the same space of images. The frame theorem of the paper tolerates loss of dimension; the exact condition above does not follow from an approximate rank bound alone. The construction does allow a naive dimension obstruction to be overcome: for the three coordinate planes V_i⊂R³ with their inclusions one has q=1,k=2, and no subset of whole planes gives a direct basis of R³. Use as coefficient vectors (1,0),(0,1),(1,1). Each coordinate appears in exactly two planes and its two coefficient vectors form a basis of R². After reordering coordinates, the map is a sum of three unimodular blocks and its determinant has modulus one.

An explicit candidate and a discarding criterion for a determinantal route have thus been constructed. It remains to check in the real arithmetic families the non-vanishing of the polynomial (or to quantify the defect on removing kernels), the conversion of metrics and the cost in the budget of the host. The example of planes certifies the linear mechanism, not those three inputs.

## Integrality threshold of the rank and construction in dimension two

In (F4) of the paper, write S=∑r_i, D=κ+d for the dimension of the augmented target and d/D≤δ. In the coordinates given by (F4), let C_i be the augmented matrices, K_i=c_i C_i* C_i and Q=∑ c_i C_i C_i*. Given U_i, write p=(∑dim U_i)/S, h=dim∑ C_i U_i, z=h/D, and t for the dimension of the image after projecting to the κ real rows of the frame (the first rows, also over the complex field). The dimensions are invariant under the corresponding changes of basis.

The trace of ∑ c_i C_i P_{U_i} C_i* is at least S(p−√(pe)), by Cauchy–Schwarz applied to K_i−1 and the projectors. That sum is supported on the space of dimension h and dominated by Q, hence its trace is ≤tr(P_h Q). Let q=tr Q. By (F4), q≤S(1+√e), and ||Q−qI/D||_HS≤q√(η/D). Since ||P_h−zI||_HS=√(Dz(1−z)), one deduces

    p ≤ √(pe)+(1+√e)[z+√(ηz(1−z))].

By h≤t+d one has z≤t/κ+δ. Thus the normalised defect p−t/κ is at most E=δ+2√e+(1+√e)√η/2. In particular, if E<gcd(S,κ)/(Sκ), all the rank inequalities of the previous section are exact: every positive defect is at least that fraction by integrality. This proves an implication verifiable on the matrices, without claiming that the families of the paper satisfy the threshold.

The rank size in the threshold cannot be omitted. With κ=2, S=2l, take l+1 inclusions of lines on the first axis and l−1 on the second, of norm one with c_i=1. Then δ=e=0 and η=1/l² suffices since Q=diag(l+1,l−1). For the first l+1 lines the defect is 1/(2l), exactly E and exactly the integrality step. The proposed square matrix is singular for every mixture. In particular the case of equality in the threshold does not suffice.

**Sufficient construction without invoking a partition theorem.** If κ=2 and all the r_i=1, the exact rank conditions exclude zero maps and say that no image line contains more than S/2 of the sources. With q=2/gcd(2,S) copies there are 2k=qS lines counted with multiplicity. Group the equal ones in a contiguous list and pair position j with j+k for 1≤j≤k. Since each group has length ≤k, each pair consists of two independent lines. Assign each pair to a copy of the target: the integral matrix (when the maps preserve the lattices) is a direct sum of k isomorphisms over the field. In each mixing column exactly one coefficient one and the others zero suffice. The summand (log k)/2 of the Hadamard bound is thus eliminated for these families. The independence property can be verified in any embedding of the common field. What is missing is an arithmetic argument that produces or reduces the data of the column criterion to this situation, or that controls the loss in the real dimensions of the problem.

## Selection by lengths and cost of the lost directions

In the model of Euclidean lattices over Z, a single copy of the target can be used, even with sources of arbitrary rank. Suppose given non-zero integral maps B_i:N_i→C, h_i≥log||B_i||, s=∑r_i(μ(N_i)−h_i)/S, μ_max(N_i)−h_i≤s+u and μ_min(C)≥s−H. Here μ_max dominates the slopes of the sublattices of rank one, and μ_min is the infimum of the slopes of saturated quotients with quotient metric. Suppose the control (F4) for the dimensions of the subspaces. In these conventions one obtains

    μ(C) ≥ s − 4(u+c) − (δ+η+6√e)H,
    c = (1/S)∑ r_i log(2/vol(Ball_{r_i})^(1/r_i)) ≤ (1/2) log(max r_i).

Proof. In N_i choose independent vectors of lengths equal to its successive minima. Write λ=−log||v||−h_i for each one. They are ≤s+u by the bound on the maximal slope. Minkowski's second theorem gives ∏||v||≤2^{r_i}covol(N_i)/vol(Ball_{r_i}); therefore the mean of the S numbers λ is ≥s−c. For the upper bound on c use the cube of side 2/√r inside the ball. One deduces ∑(s−λ)_+/S≤u+c.

For any threshold, let p be the fraction of vectors with λ above the threshold and z the fraction of the augmented dimension occupied by their images. The trace of Q on the complement (fraction w=1−z) is ≤S(1−p+√e), by the estimate of K_i−1 on the complement of the chosen source subspaces. By near-scalarity it is ≥(tr Q)(w−√(ηw)); moreover tr Q≥S(1−√e). It follows, also if the parenthesis is negative,

    w ≤ (1−p+√e)/(1−√e)+√(ηw)
      ≤ 2(1−p)+2√e+w/2+η/2.

If t is the rank in the projected target, 1−t/κ≤w/(1−δ); to avoid a denominator one also has 1−t/κ≤w+δ/(1−δ). More directly, using t≥zD−d, one obtains z−t/κ≤d/D≤δ (if zD<d, use z≤δ). Consequently 1−t/κ≤4(1−p)+4√e+η+δ. This implies the somewhat looser constant of the statement.

Order the vectors from larger to smaller λ, retaining an image only if it enlarges the space spanned, and stop at λ=s−H (the equalities do not affect the integral). For each threshold between s−H and s, the fraction of dimensions retained above the threshold is t/κ: it is the elementary property of selection of a basis by independence. The degree of the saturated lattice spanned by the retained images is at least the sum of their λ, by Hadamard and integrality; saturation only increases the degree. The quotient has slope ≥s−H by hypothesis. Adding the degrees and truncating the λ greater than s to obtain a minorant, one gets

    s−μ(C) ≤ ∫_{s−H}^s (1−t/κ) d threshold
             ≤ 4(u+c)+(δ+η+4√e)H.

One uses that the integral of (1−p) on that interval is bounded by the mean of (s−λ)_+. It is not required that all the selected vectors of one and the same source reach the target together. The bound proves in this model a loss linear in u plus the cost of successive minima. We shall not use this statement directly with the Hermitian modules of the paper: the heights by place, the change of coordinates and the discriminant cost have to be transported; moreover, the term δH may exceed the budget of the original frame. These two questions affect the arithmetic use of the result.

In fact the first of the two projection bounds keeps the correct budget. Since κ−t≤D−zD, using 1−t/κ≤w/(1−δ) **before** integrating gives

    s−μ(C) ≤ [4(u+c)+(η+4√e)H]/(1−δ).

Thus the additive cost δH disappears also with lost rank. The Hermitian conversion and the cost c still require control.

## Direct transfer to the Hermitian frame theorem

**Proposition.** Under exactly (F1)–(F4) of thm:frameF in paper 46 one has

    s−μ(CC) ≤ [4Hu/(H+u) + (η+4√e)H]/(1−δ),

interpreting the quotient as zero if H=u=0. The estimate holds over the original number field. The preceding Euclidean lemma is not used, nor are successive minima compared with slopes.

**Proof.** In the notations of steps 1–4 of thm:frameF, give uniform weight to the n_K∑r_i numbers X=2λ_{ij}+c_K−h_i of the sources and to the n_Kκ numbers X'=2λ'_j+c_K of the target, in their respective probability spaces. (F1) and (F2) give E X≥s and X≤s+u. By (F3), X'≥s−H. For each τ∈[0,H], apply prop:frameLA with the real defect ε(τ)=Pr[X<s−τ], which is exactly the relative corank of the HN pieces of the sources. The rest of the proof of the paper is kept: the height inequality takes their images to the piece of the target with threshold s−τ, and projecting from the augmented target costs at most the factor 1/(1−δ) in the relative corank. Hence

    Pr[X'<s−τ] ≤ [η+4√e+4 Pr[X<s−τ]]/(1−δ).

On integrating, the contribution of the sources is E min(H,(s−X)_+). For any X≤s+u, with H+u>0,

    min(H,(s−X)_+) ≤ [H/(H+u)] (s+u−X).

For X≥s it is immediate; for X<s the quotient min(H,d)/(u+d) with d=s−X attains its maximum at d=H (with the case u=0 by continuity). Taking means and using E X≥s gives the bound Hu/(H+u). In the target, s−μ(CC)≤E(s−X')_+=∫₀ᴴ Pr[X'<s−τ]dτ. This proves the proposition, including H=0 or u=0. The c_K and the heights are exactly those of the transport of thresholds of the paper. The additional loss c of the successive minima does not appear.

The improvement is uniform because v/(1+v)≤log(1+v) for v≥0. For H≫u the dispersion term stays ≤4u instead of growing like 4u log(H/u). More precisely, 4Hu/(H+u) can be replaced by four times the truncated mean of the deficits of the sources if that mean is available directly. In the proposition prop:pointwise, the term 4 log(1+x^{c_HH})(K₃+[ℓ]+b) coming from P8 can be replaced by 4(K₃+[ℓ]) when P8 is obtained from this frame and the other hypotheses are kept. This follows from ū≤K₃+[ℓ] and Hū/(H+ū)≤ū. The error term (η+4√e)H persists, as does the need to construct the maps and to satisfy (F4) in the concrete families. The improvement does not prove those inputs.

## What correlations the slope step really needs

In the augmented target of (F4) absorb √c_i into the matrices C_i and put Q=∑C_i C_i*, q=tr Q>0, d'=dim(augmented target). For the HN pieces at threshold s−τ, let P_i(τ) be the orthogonal projector onto the high source in the coordinates of (F4). Define the loss weighted by the maps themselves,

    L = q⁻¹ ∫₀ᴴ ∑_i tr[(I−P_i(τ)) C_i* C_i] dτ,
    η_Q = d' tr(Q²)/q² − 1.

With the hypotheses of transport of thresholds and projection of thm:frameF one obtains directly

    (1−δ)(s−μ(CC)) ≤ 2L + η_Q H.

Indeed, for the projector P onto the complement of the sum of high images, w=tr(P)/d', one has PC_iP_i=0 and therefore tr(PQ)≤∑ tr[(I−P_i)C_i*C_i]. By Cauchy–Schwarz against P−wI, tr(PQ)/q≥w−√(η_Q w). Integrating and putting W=∫w gives W≤L+√(η_Q H W)≤L+(W+η_Q H)/2. The projected corank costs at most w/(1−δ); the lower bound s−H in the target authorises the same layer argument as before. This version is invariant under simultaneous rescaling of all the C_i. In particular η_Q is read off from a single sum of correlations, tr(Q²)=∑_{i,j}||C_i*C_j||²_HS, including the diagonal terms. The final inequality does not require each pair to be bounded separately.

To compare it with the parameters of the paper, let A=E min(H,(s−X)_+)≤Hu/(H+u) with X as in the previous section. The integral of the sum of the ranks of I−P_i, divided by S, is A. If ∑||C_i*C_i−I||²_HS≤eS, q≥S(1−√e), and the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality in the indices and then in τ gives

    L ≤ [A+√(eHA)]/(1−√e).

Here one uses ||I−P_i||²_HS=rank(I−P_i). For e≤1/4 one concludes

    (1−δ)(s−μ(CC)) ≤ 4A+4√(eHA)+η_Q H ≤ 6A+(2e+η_Q)H.

Thus, when the budget allows A of order B, eH and η_Q H of order B suffice in the slope step (with 1−δ bounded away from zero). If A=0 the fitting defect in the source costs nothing directly. The parameter η_Q may still depend on the diagonal errors when the correlations are computed; the saving must not be carried over to the hypothesis LinkS without bounding that dependence.

The annex (definition i3:def-linkS and remark i3:rem-window) provides a stronger mechanism, of pairwise comparisons, conditional on a window of ranks of the words. Outside that window the implication LinkS may be empty. The alternative route must construct the maps on the same hosts and effectively control Q and L there. The trace identity says which aggregate estimates would be sufficient even if some individual pairs were bad.

## A criterion on the images only, even with large eigenvalues

At the auxiliary place of the frame theorem, take directly the images E_i of the word maps B_i in the target of dimension κ. In the annex there appear maps of Poincaré series P_u and normalised blocks (c₀(u)c₀(v))^(−1/2) P_u*P_v (paragraph «Column data» before i3:S-prop-transport). The identification between the augmented spectral datum and the first rows of the frame must be respected: if one starts from the augmented one, one first projects to the target of the column. The following computation can be applied directly to the resulting images. The norms that transport the slopes remain the original ones. The dimensional argument admits any common auxiliary Hermitian product on the target.

Let Π_i be the orthogonal projection onto E_i, S=∑r_i and V=(κ/S)∑Π_i. For α>0 let b_α be the fraction of eigenvalues of V strictly less than α, with multiplicity. With the hypotheses (F1)–(F3), the integrality and the heights of the maps that allow the thresholds to be transported, one has directly

    s−μ(CC) ≤ b_α H + A/α,     A=E min(H,(s−X)_+).

Proof: for a threshold let U_i be the high sources, Z the orthogonal complement of ∑B_i U_i and ε the fraction of dimensions discarded in the sources. Since B_i U_i⊂E_i and Z is perpendicular to B_i U_i, tr(P_Z Π_i)≤dim E_i−dim B_i U_i≤r_i−dim U_i. Summing, tr(P_Z V)/κ≤ε. If P is the spectral projector of V onto [α,∞), then V≥αP and tr(P_Z P)≥dim Z−κ b_α. It follows that dim Z/κ≤b_α+ε/α. By HN transport the fraction of low slopes of the target does not exceed that quantity. Integrating in τ∈[0,H] completes the proof. It includes maps with kernel, and the projector of a zero map is zero.

Thus, if α is absolute and b_α H fits in the budget, for this step neither near-scalarity nor isometric fitting of the maps is needed; coverage in the low part of the spectrum of the sum of projectors is required. The criterion is invariant under individual rescalings and under changes of basis in each source. The difference is seen even in dimension two: three lines on the first axis and one on the second, with ranks one, give V=diag(3/2,1/2). With α=1/2 one obtains s−μ(CC)≤2A for any H. The scalar defect of that same matrix is 1/4 and does not tend to zero. It has not been proved that the images of P_u, after the projection and the identification with the integral maps of the host, satisfy a uniform bound for b_α. The symmetry by isomorphisms of the annex preserves the correlations, but is not enough to conclude such coverage.

## Transfer to the good rows by means of the Gram matrix

In the concrete spectral datum of the annex the blocks K_ij=C_i* C_j with C_i=P_i/√c₀(i) are known formally. It is possible to express a sufficient certificate of coverage **with that Gram alone**, without characterising individually the rows that each period sees. Let D be the dimension of the complete target, S the sum of dimensions of the sources and κ the dimension of the rows that are kept. Suppose that the isometric identification between the kept rows and the auxiliary target of the frame has been established, so that their images are the projections of im C_i, up to common changes of basis in the target and individual invertible ones in the sources. We can transport the auxiliary product by the common change of basis. This compatibility hypothesis must be verified in each realisation.

Construct J=diag((K_ii)^†/²), where the notation indicates the positive root of the pseudoinverse: on the kernel one takes zero. Then the list of the positive eigenvalues of (D/S) J K J is exactly the positive list of V₀=(D/S)∑Π_im C_i. Indeed, C_i(K_ii)^†/² is a partial isometry onto im C_i; on concatenating those matrices, the two operators are the products of the matrix by its adjoint in opposite order, with the same scale. To compute the fraction b₀(α) of eigenvalues of V₀ less than α>0 one has to count D−r zeros, where r=rank K, in addition to the positive ones less than α. Using S−r would give the wrong dimension.

Let P be the orthogonal projection onto the κ good rows. For each subspace E, one has Π_PE ≥ PΠ_E P* in that target: the compression is a positive contraction with image in PE. Thus V≥(κ/D)P V₀ P*. By the variational principle, if V₀ has j eigenvalues less than α, its remaining spectral subspace meets the good target in dimension at least κ−j. Consequently

    b_{ακ/D}(V) ≤ (D/κ)b₀(α),
    s−μ(CC) ≤ (D/κ)[b₀(α)H + A/α].

The second formula uses the result on thresholds of the previous section. The orientation of the removed rows does not require an additional estimate. Only the quotient D/κ and the spectral count of the complete Gram matter. The matrix J amplifies the small singular directions, which is why the exact Gram is used here; approximate data would require rank control or a certified singular cut.

**Arithmetic scope.** The proposition i3:prop-irr-row of the annex works under the automorphic identification (AT) and obtains, with other inputs, a row-count datum. Step R4 of i3:str-allrows adds dimensions of labels by means of orthogonal central idempotents. That count does not determine J K J: two maps of lines to a plane may have the same dimensions and norms, but if their images coincide the normalised Gram has rank one, and if they are orthogonal it has rank two. We do not infer commutation of the projectors of images with the idempotents of the labels. What is still missing is either the low-spectrum bound for the concrete K, or a sufficient estimate of their correlations that implies it on the hosts of the construction.

## Effective difference between the level-zero rank and the division rank

The annex warns after i4:lem-proviso that it uses two dimensions with close notations. The window in Γ controls d^S_u=dim M₀(u), while the weights in the slope s are the ranks d_u of the division lattices. In i4:Llem-lvl(a), under the level inputs indicated there, one obtains d_u=d^S_u(1−1/(T_u−1))^f. It is important to use the dimension of the space on which each Gram acts: an equality on dim Vπ(u) by itself does not identify all the class spaces with the division lattices.

Let S₀=∑d^S_u, S=∑d_u and λ=S/S₀. If T_min>2 is the smallest level, Bernoulli gives 0≤1−λ≤f/(T_min−1). The pseudoinverse can be avoided when transferring a trace estimate from level zero, under the following identification: the division sources are realised as subspaces of those of level zero and the projection of the maps onto their high pieces is the arithmetic image of the frame (equality of the image subspaces for each threshold suffices). Knowing only the dimensions does not give this identification.

In the complete target of dimension D let Q₀=∑ C_i C_i* be formed with the level-zero sources, K_ij=C_i*C_j, q=tr Q₀, e=∑||K_ii−I||²/S₀, η=D tr(Q₀²)/q²−1. Here and in this paragraph the matrix norms are Hilbert–Schmidt. If e≤1/4, the integrated average of the discarded ranks, using the denominator S₀, is exactly A₀=λA+(1−λ)H: at each threshold all the omitted dimensions are discarded before passing to the high pieces. The preceding trace proof uses only those ranks and the projection to the good target. Consequently

    s−μ(CC) ≤ (D/κ)[6λA+6(1−λ)H+(2e+η)H].

The specific loss due to the discrepancy of dimensions, inside the bracket, is ≤6fH/(T_min−1). On the hosts with H≤x^{C_H}b and T_min≥b^{m/2}, b=exp(x^ε), that loss is ≤12 f x^{C_H} b^{1−m/2} for b^{m/2}≥2. The comparison is made on the same host and does not use an extrapolation of the window of Γ.

To audit η as well without confusing weights, if the aggregate estimate ∑_{i≠j}||K_ij||² ≤(1+v)S₀²/D_* is available then

    1+η ≤ [(D/S₀)(1+√e)² + (D/D_*)(1+v)]/(1−√e)².

Indeed q≥S₀(1−√e), and the sum of the squares of the diagonal blocks is ≤S₀(1+√e)² by the triangle inequality in the direct sum of matrix spaces. In particular a bound on the number of words from above only is not enough to neglect D/S₀. The annex gives the size of the choice in the level argument (see the expression with ceiling immediately after the bound for log g_i in the construction of words). The aggregate estimate of the correlations and the identification of subspaces on the effective hosts remain arithmetic inputs.

## Matrix identity that transports all the thresholds

In the source of the reconstruction (TopDensityNGeSixteen.lean, definitions MatS_at and MatReal_at), the relation is written before any threshold is chosen. For one and the same place v, with invertible changes of basis Y_i and Y', projection π to the first κ rows, complete matrices B_i^f and rectangular matrices V_i, it is

    V_i*V_i=I,    π B_i^f V_i = (Y')⁻¹ σ_v(B_i) Y_i.

The domain of B_i^f has dimension d_i^S and that of V_i has dimension d_i. Therefore, for the HN piece U_i(τ) in arithmetic coordinates, the level-zero subsource is V_i Y_i⁻¹ U_i(τ). It has exactly dim U_i(τ), and its projected image coincides with (Y')⁻¹σ_v(B_i)U_i(τ). Thus the simultaneous transport and the term 1−λ of the previous section are justified provided the witnesses of the matrix identity are constructed. The result frameReal_at_of receives MatReal_at as a hypothesis; matRealF_of deduces the uniform version of MatSF using the decay of its errors. Locating those implications does not construct their arithmetic witnesses.

There is a relaxation for the trace route. If the matrix equality holds only modulo a common subspace E of the good target, of dimension l, then for each threshold the sum of the projected images is contained in the sum of arithmetic images plus E. Consequently the arithmetic corank can exceed the corank estimated by the trace by at most l. Integrating adds lH/κ to the right-hand side of the slope bound. It suffices to take E as the span of all the columns of the difference matrices (with the same changes of basis for all the thresholds). It is not enough for each difference to have small rank separately if their images fill the whole target. This version allows an identity to be proved in a common quotient of controlled codimension; it remains pending to realise it with its budget and to obtain the aggregate correlation.

## From the periods to the matrices and what can be weakened

In GlobalTheoremInputs.lean, BesselId consists of rationality of the forms B_u and the identity B_u(φ,F)=a_u besS_u(U_u φ,V F) for all φ,F, with a_u non-zero, a unitary isometry U_u per word and **one and the same** isometry V on the rows. The lemma blockReal_at_of of TopDensityNGeSixteen.lean produces the witnesses of BlockReal_at from that statement. The inclusion iotaC of the ramified word in level zero is isometric; besS is defined as the inner product with its image by P_u. In orthonormal coordinates of both domains, the matrix of iotaC composed with U_u has orthonormal columns and gives V_u. A single orthonormal basis of the good rows, transported with V and the antiunitary identification of rows, is extended to the complete target. Multiplying the matrix of P_u by V_u and taking its first rows evaluates the period in those bases. The Bessel identity and the rationality identify those entries with those of the integral map, after the changes of basis and the factor a_u. The choice between the embedding and its conjugate is made in common; conjugating simultaneously preserves the Hilbert–Schmidt norms. Thus the witnesses are constructed conditionally on BesselId and on the basis data of the host. matK_at_of then incorporates the positivity of the normalisations and the estimates of the blocks; matKF_of uses LinkDIV for BesselId and LinkS together with the proviso for the estimates. The identity of periods is an input separate from its conversion to matrices.

**A sufficient condition on images.** With the place, the arithmetic identifications and the ramified isometric inclusions fixed, let A_u be the arithmetic matrix maps and C_u the spectral ones already restricted and projected, both with domains of the same dimension per word. For the compatibility required in our argument it suffices that there exist a single complex isomorphism L of the targets such that L(im C_u)=im A_u for each u. Indeed, two maps to the same space, with equal image and with domains of equal dimension, differ by an isomorphism of their domains: identify their kernels by means of a basis and, on complements, transport by the bijective restrictions to the common image. Thus there are invertible changes of basis Y_u with LC_u=A_u Y_u, and each HN piece is transported by applying Y_u⁻¹ before the spectral inclusion. The auxiliary change in the source does not alter the arithmetic height, which is measured beforehand in the original metrics. This criterion omits the individual values of the periods and the unitary character of the changes of source. A single L for all the words, and the rationality, integrality and height of the A_u, remain essential. Neither the equality of dimensions nor an identification L_u differing from word to word implies that simultaneity.

## The change of rows comes from a witness prior to the choice of word

The sequence of the source is kernelJoin_of_multiplicity → kerJoinS_of_joinAt2 → besselId_of_joins (files FlagStabilizerTensors/AdmissibleOfTransported.lean and KerJoinS.lean). In the first implication the row operator V₁ is chosen from the common transformation of the chain and from its compatibility with conjugation, before fixing u. Then one obtains a column isometry and a scalar per word. The proof uses MIstar2, the identification of the chain, the surjectivity onto the row space, and the compatibilities of rows, column norms and pairings. In the last step, given a row vector x, it is lifted by the surjective map ι; a lift orthogonal to the indicated kernel in the initial space can be chosen by subtracting its orthogonal projection, without changing x. The equality of periods on those lifts and the dictionary of columns give the equality for every x. The result of this step in the source is called adm13; rationality is another clause of BesselId.

**Linear descent without choosing isometries.** For the alternative by images there is a way of measuring precisely the cost of not having that unitary witness. Let a:Z→E and b:Z→F be two linear epimorphisms, with dim E=dim F=κ, on one and the same finite comparison space. Let U_i⊂Z be such that their images by a and b are respectively the spectral and arithmetic images of the word i (after converting both period spaces to the same duality convention). Write K=ker a, J=ker b and l=dim K−dim(K∩J). Both quotients E/a(J) and F/b(K) are canonically Z/(K+J), and dim a(J)=dim b(K)=l. Therefore there exists an isomorphism L:E→F, common to all the indices, such that b−La has image in b(K). Indeed one lifts the isomorphism of quotients on respective complements and chooses an isomorphism of the two spaces of dimension l. The bound is optimal in the problem of comparing **the maps**: (b−La)(K)=b(K) for every L.

If moreover source identifications are available such that both maps per word are obtained by applying a and b to the same map Z_i→Z, the common matrix error has rank ≤l and costs lH/κ by the preceding lemma. With only the equality of images modulo that subspace, it suffices that the domains have equal dimension: the two maps to the quotient have the same image and therefore become equal after a change of basis in the source, as in the previous section. The result is useful only for an arithmetically fixed Z with the restrictions per word: taking Z=E⊕F with the two projections gives l=κ and loses all the height. On the chosen comparison space, l=rank((a,b):Z→E⊕F)−κ; in this way the condition becomes a single simultaneous rank. A bound for that rank with admissible loss has not been established for the automorphic hosts.

## A minor of the arithmetic columns can certify the slope step

There is an alternative to the transport of rows if one works directly with the integral maps of the frame theorem. Suppose κ>0 and keep the height control, the inclusion of the lattices and (F3). For each i one takes a basis over K adapted to the flag formed by the K-spans of the high pieces of N_i of step 1 of thm:frameF. To each vector of that basis one assigns the value x=2λ+c_K−h_i of the jump at which it appears. In the case of a multiple jump each added vector receives that value. This x labels the piece of the filtration; it does not require knowing the length of the basis vector. Its image by B_i is a column with cost d=min(H,max(0,s−x)). The κ columns of an arbitrary basis of W are added at cost H.

For a selection J of κ columns let Δ_J be its determinant in a basis of W. Then

    s−μ(CC) ≤ (1/κ) min_{Δ_J ≠ 0} ∑_{j∈J} d_j .

Indeed, for 0≤τ<H the rank r(τ) of the columns of cost ≤τ is precisely the rank over K of the sum of images of the high pieces at threshold s−τ. The transport by height of step 2 of the theorem gives dim_K CC(τ)≥r(τ), where the dimension denotes that of its span. By (F3) and integration, s−μ(CC)≤∫₀ᴴ(κ−r(τ))dτ/κ. Order the columns by increasing cost and accept one each time it raises the rank. The filler columns guarantee that κ is reached. For each τ<H exactly r(τ) columns of cost ≤τ have been accepted: the accepted ones are independent and span all those seen up to that cost. Any other basis extracted from the list contains at most r(τ) columns of cost ≤τ. Integrating these counts shows that the integral is exactly the indicated minimum. If H=0 the claim results directly from (F3).

In particular, it suffices to exhibit t independent arithmetic columns whose costs add up to D. They are completed from the auxiliary basis and the total loss is at most (D+(κ−t)H)/κ. A non-zero minor of order t after reduction at a finite place, in integral coordinates there, establishes that independence in characteristic zero. The height of the auxiliary basis vectors does not enter the estimate. The claim also makes precise why a full-rank determinant of the unlabelled maps is not enough: it may use only vectors that appear in the last jumps. The minimisation expressed here is the elementary argument of the minimum-weight basis applied to the arithmetic flags of the paper. To obtain the bound on G it still remains to construct or bound those minors of low cost, with the budget of the average in the effective arithmetic data, in addition to the other inputs of the joint slope inequality.

## What the reduction at a finite place would have to span

The proof in the annex of i4:Llem-lvl(b) establishes fullness and commensurability in the domain of each word. The unit minor of i2:lem-blockring serves to compose a presentation with a left factor while keeping its kernel. The minor in the proof of exactness of the Breuil–Kisin modules comes from a split sequence of lattices. None of these three statements estimates the image in the same target of a set of division maps B_i. In i4:prop-links-formal, clause c5, the object to be related is the period B_u(φ,x). The difference can be proved even assuming that the union of all the maps is integrally surjective.

**Example with the metrics of the theorem.** Take K=Q(i), the target O_K² with orthogonal axes of lengths 1 and exp(L/2), L>0, at the complex place. Let there be m≥2 copies of the first axis and one of the second, with the induced metrics; the B_i are their inclusions. All have height zero, primitive image and rank one at every finite place. The sum map is split surjective over O_K, and has a minor equal to one. Let μ₀ be the normalised slope of the axis of length one. The axes have slopes μ₀ and μ₀−L by rescaling; as real lattices they are square copies of Z[i] at the respective scales. The flag by axes has semistable graded pieces of decreasing slopes, so it is the HN flag of the target. In particular μ(CC)=μ₀−L/2 and μ_min(CC)=μ₀−L. In (F1)–(F3) we can take

    s=μ₀−L/(m+1),     u=L/(m+1),     H=mL/(m+1).

With L=m+1 one gets u=1 and s−μ(CC)=(m−1)/2. No uniform budget O(u) follows from the joint surjectivity, even if it is certified by a unit minor at all primes and all the maps are isometries onto their images. In this example only one domain, precisely the one of lowest slope, spans the second direction.

**A certificate that avoids computing the flags.** Let S=∑r_i and suppose that g>0 disjoint groups of words I_j are given. Denote by κ−a_j the rank over K of the concatenation of their B_i, before imposing thresholds. A lower bound for that rank after reduction modulo one prime per group, which may vary between groups, suffices. Let A be the mean of min(H,(s−X)_+) of the domains, with X and its multiplicities as in thm:frameF. Then

    s−μ(CC) ≤ H (∑_j a_j)/(gκ) + [S/(gκ)] A.

Indeed, at each threshold, replacing within the group each source by its high piece can lower the rank of the group by at most the sum of the codimensions removed in it, by the map induced from the direct sum of the quotients. The image of all the high pieces contains that of each group. Therefore its corank is bounded by the mean of the a_j plus the mean of the codimensions removed. By disjointness, the sum of the latter over the groups does not exceed the sum over all the sources. Integrating over the thresholds and using step 2 of thm:frameF gives the formula. Under (F1)–(F2), A≤Hu/(H+u) with the previous convention.

Thus, g surjective groups with g≥c S/κ for a common c>0 would suffice for this step with loss at most u/c. One would only have to exhibit ordinary minors on disjoint sets of **complete words**, even with different primes. In the example every surjective grouping consumes the only word of the second axis; necessarily g≤1. To apply the arithmetic improvement what is missing is an estimate of how many groups of words of the real hosts simultaneously span the required rows, or of their mean corank within the budget. The number of words given in «Level primes and words» of the annex does not provide that estimate.

## How much coverage is needed in a sample of words

The proposition prop:pointwise of 46 admits a logarithmic loss in the dispersion of the sources. This allows the packing into disjoint groups to be replaced by a uniform sample with replacement. Let N be the number of words, S the sum of the division ranks, κ the rank of the target and A the truncated mean deficit of the sources. For a random list of h independent and uniform words let a_ar be its joint corank, computed with the complete arithmetic maps. Then

    s−μ(CC) ≤ H E[a_ar]/κ + h S A/(Nκ).

Indeed, at each threshold the loss of rank of the list is bounded by the sum of the codimensions removed in the h sources, even when one is repeated. In expectation this sum is h/N times that of all the sources. The high images of the list are contained in the high piece of the target. Integration gives the formula above. A corank computed at a finite place in integral coordinates also serves, since it is an upper bound for the corank over the number field.

**The same sample in the spectral realisation.** Its corank can be estimated without a conjecture of independence of the periods. Let C_i be the normalised level-zero maps, D the complete dimension, S₀ the sum of dimensions of their sources and G_i the ramified subspaces (of total codimension ΔS₀, with Δ=1−S/S₀). One projects to κ orthogonal rows. Suppose

    ∑ ||C_i* C_i−I||²_HS = e S₀,    e≤1/4,
    Q=∑ C_i C_i*,    q=tr Q,    η=D tr(Q²)/q²−1.

Define z as the corank of the projected images already seen divided by D, and Z as their complement within the κ rows, considered in the complete target. By Cauchy–Schwarz against P_Z−zI,

    tr(P_Z Q) ≥ q(z−√(ηz)) ≥ q(z/2−η/2).

Moreover S₀/2≤q≤3S₀/2, by the first hypothesis. For each i one has

    tr(P_Z C_i C_i*) ≤ 2 rank(P_Z C_i|G_i) + 2 codim G_i + ||C_i* C_i−I||²_HS.

Indeed, on truncating the eigenvalues of C_i C_i* at 2, the compression of the truncated operator has norm ≤2 and rank at most rank(P_Z C_i). The excess of trace is bounded by the sum of (λ−2)_+≤(λ−1)² over the positive eigenvalues. Restricting to G_i reduces the rank by at most its codimension.

The next increment of rank is precisely rank(P_Z C_i|G_i). Summing the last inequality and taking the uniform mean, one obtains

    E[z_next | Z] ≤ (1−p)z + p B,
    p=S₀/(8ND),     B=3η+4e+8Δ.

Here 0<p<1: the kernels of the C_i contribute at most eS₀ dimensions, so that (1−e)S₀≤ND. Iterating from z=κ/D≤1 gives E[a_esp]/D≤B+exp(−ph). With X=x^{c_HH} and h=ceil(log(1+X)/p), the last term costs at most bD/κ in the slope inequality, by H≤Xb. The cost of the discards is at most (D/κ)[8log(1+X)+4/3]A, using S≤S₀ and S₀≤4ND/3. Both costs have the order allowed in prop:pointwise when D/κ is bounded; the additional cost HB requires its own estimate, with η, e and Δ on the effective family. Under the counting statements and the proviso of the annex, D is comparable to D_* and S₀/N to D_*/Γ², so that h is of the order of Γ² log(1+X), up to the rounding. The number of samples may exceed N; all the formulas use replacement.

The cost HB can be made precise in the concrete choice of the code. Under the spectral inputs LS of the annex, write t=exp(−x^{ε/8}); there √e, η₀, η₁≤t. For the injective code of length N=ceil(3Γ²x^{C_F}), the proviso gives S₀≥ND_*/(2Γ²), so that D/S₀≤(2/3)(1+t)x^{−C_F}. The inequality for 1+η of «Effective difference between the level-zero rank and the division rank» gives η=O(x^{−C_F}+t), taking v=η₀ and D/D_*≤1+t (if some upper bound η_j is negative it suffices to replace it by t). By i4:Llem-lvl, Δ≤f/(T_min−1), with T_min≥b^{m/2}. In the kits f is polynomial in x and m≥1; therefore x^{C_H}Δ and x^{C_H}t tend to zero for fixed ε. In the choice C_F=C_H and c_HH=C_H of the annex, H≤x^{C_H}b gives HB=O(b). Thus, in that construction the spectral cost of the lost rows fits in the budget, conditionally on the LS and level estimates that have just been used.

**What comparison remains.** For this step it suffices to bound from above E[a_ar]−E[a_esp] for that single list size, with acceptable loss after multiplying by H/κ. The two expectations can be taken separately, even at different places. With c5 and the complete integral identifications, the ranks of each list coincide and the difference is zero. An autonomous deduction that dispenses with c5 must still dominate that difference or directly E[a_ar]; the preceding Gram estimates control E[a_esp]. Thus the information that the normalised blocks do provide is quantified, without attributing it to the arithmetic ranks.

## A count per word in the matrices of rational periods

In the rational construction of columns there is indeed an object prior to the spectral comparison that allows the count to be set up. In the notation of Columns/FiniteSumNonzeroLargeDepth, BtC applies the pairing PtilFF to the column colC(φ), with φ in the ramified source, and to a row F in V₀. For a fixed tuple, level and section of classes, it has the expression over the coefficient field K

    Bt_u(φ,F) = ∑_c wt(c)⁻¹ pairBK(brC_u(colC_u(φ)(d_c)), F(d_c)).

Here d_c is the representative of the class c. The formula comes from BtOf, PtilFF and GSD.csOf; PtilFF_eq in InterlaceDifferenceShiftInvariant identifies that sum with the period after lowering the weight. V₀ is the image of the operator goodAt in RowForms. BkOf descends to the quotient V₀/U if the periods vanish on U. At the base level, PairingFactorsThroughPeriods uses the scalar ζ(u,μ₀)=1. To apply the matrices of the model to the division data one must have the rational identification of the columns and a single identification of the rows, as in ColTie.Bk of RowSideTie. Having the definition of BkOf does not verify those identifications on all the hosts.

Suppose those identifications made on the host considered. One chooses F₁,…,Fκ in V₀ that project to a basis of the rows of the dual target, and a basis of each source. Form the matrix M_u with row j equal to Bt_u(·,F_j) evaluated on that source basis. Changing the lifts by elements of U leaves all the matrices the same. It is not necessary to choose a spectral basis. One can work over K, or for **all** the words in the same residue field, after choosing integral coordinates of the periods. In this second case one uses only that the rank of the concatenated matrix over K is at least that of its reduction. The reduction of the periods computed in integral coordinates can be done after the sum if the individual summands have denominators.

For each u mark j if the first j rows of M_u have greater rank than the first j−1. Let m_j be the number of words that mark j. In the uniform sample of h words with replacement one has the direct arithmetic bound

    E[a_ar] ≤ ∑_{j=1}^κ (1−m_j/N)^h .

Indeed, if the word u marks j, there exists a combination of its columns with first j−1 entries zero and entry j equal to one: row j does not belong to the span of the previous ones. In the concatenation of the sample one chooses one of those combinations for each j marked at least once. They are independent by their first non-zero indices. The probability that no instance marks j is (1−m_j/N)^h. The sum of those probabilities is the expected number of unmarked indices and bounds the corank from above. Independence is required only between the draws of words.

In particular, if except for a fraction ξ of the indices one has m_j/N≥c S/(Nκ), with c>0 fixed, take h=ceil((Nκ/(cS))log(1+X)), X=x^{C_H}. The loss of the slope step is then at most

    H ξ + b + [c⁻¹log(1+X)+S/(Nκ)] A

when H≤Xb. This is obtained by substituting in the preceding sampling formula and using (1−p)^h≤exp(−ph). In the comparable ranks of the annex, S/(Nκ) is of order Γ⁻², and ξ=O(x^{−C_H}) would suffice. The count requires only ranks of prefixes of matrices of **one word** in one and the same flag of rows. Each word contributes exactly rank M_u marks, which fixes the total, but not its distribution by index.

There is a limit even if one is allowed to choose the flag. For N distinct lines in dimension two, each M_u has a single column. Only the line that annihilates the first coordinate can mark the second index. Thus m₂≤1 for any flag, although two distinct lines already span the whole target. The criterion may lose the independence that appears only on combining words. In the periods of the annex, separating the summands by classes does not automatically give the marks either: to certify j the complete pairing must vanish on each previous row with **the same** combination of columns. A uniform estimate of those marks (or directly of the sample corank) for the arithmetic Bk of the hosts remains open.

There is an explicit factor of the level that is eliminated exactly from the rank problem. In brC one takes brAt with t_τ=emb_τ(conj(π_{T_u})); by the definition of brAt,

    brC_u = d(u) brStd_u,     d(u)=∏_τ t_τ^{−nBr(λ_{u,τ},σ_τ)}.

Since π_{T_u} has non-zero prime norm, d(u) is a non-zero scalar in K, common to all the classes, rows and columns of the word. By bilinearity it comes out of the complete sum. Dividing M_u by d(u) keeps each prefix rank and the rank of each concatenation over K; to certify by reduction this normalisation can be made before taking integral coordinates, also when the valuation of d(u) is not zero. The heights of the HN transport are still measured in the original maps. Moreover, brAtTranspose allows the row F_j to be computed using pairBK in the weight λ_u against ioStd_u(F_j(d_c)), after removing d(u). Therefore the values of that local power cannot by themselves separate rows of one and the same word. The dependence on the level in colC_u, its spaces, the weight and the evaluation points still counts. This cancellation does follow from the formula for the period, independently of c5.

## A right inverse on the space of values of each class

In characteristic zero, for interlaced weights with the same shift by determinant, the operator brStd on the complete modules of values is surjective. There is an explicit section on the basis of tableaux, which makes precise what part of the rank computation can already be done before studying colC. The lifting of bideterminants is that of bmLift_exists in BranchSandwichDivisors/BranchIsometryProofs/BranchMap; the basis used is in i1:thm-stdbasis of the annex and in bVVfun_spec. We give the argument in order to identify exactly the section of the operator that appears in the period.

Work in a single factor of weights. On subtracting the common shift, write λ and σ for the partitions of lengths n+1 and n, with last part zero. The interlacing λ_i≥σ_i≥λ_{i+1} says that λ/σ has at most one box in each column. For a semistandard tableau T of shape σ with entries from 1 to n, all the new boxes are filled with the letter n+1. The result is a semistandard tableau T⁺ of shape λ: in each row the new boxes are on the right, and in the columns they occupy only the last position. Let f_T be the product of initial minors that has as row indices the entries of each column of T. The last column of the matrix of variables of size n+1 is not used, because the columns of the diagram λ have height at most n.

Write z_j=X_{n+1,j} and b_j=λ_j−σ_j, with indices from one. In a lengthened column of final height j, the expansion along the last row has a term z_j times the original minor, with positive sign; the other terms contain z_l with l<j. The columns that are not lengthened contain no z. There are exactly b_j lengthened columns of height j. Giving weight l to z_l, the term that chooses the last cofactor in each column has maximal total weight, and any different choice strictly decreases it. Consequently

    [z₁^{b₁}⋯z_n^{b_n}] f_{T⁺} = f_T.

This holds over Z, also for originally empty columns. Extending T↦T⁺ by linearity one obtains a section on the basis of tableaux for the raw extraction on its chosen image. For the complete operator one takes the tensor product over the places. The extraction returns the required vector and therefore the projection projVV fixes it. In characteristic zero the constant brConst is non-zero under the interlacing and the common shift (brConst_ne in InterlaceDifferenceShiftInvariant). Dividing the lift by that constant gives the section of brStd; dividing moreover by d(u) gives that of brC. For the weights of the chain, sameShift_of_interlace deduces the common shift from the equality of its last two upper entries and the interlacing. The integral identity refers to the raw extraction on these tableaux; the divisions and the projection do not authorise transporting the same integral statement for the normalised map at an arbitrary prime.

**Consequence for the sum over classes.** Consider a fixed set of class representatives and the ambient space of all the assignments of values to them. Given any system of low values one obtains a system of high values by applying the section class by class. Thus the pointwise branching operator fills the whole low ambient space. More explicitly, if R is a complement of U in V₀, let E be the evaluation of R at the representatives that the sum uses. With unrestricted high assignments one reaches exactly the functionals on R that vanish on ker E. Indeed, these are functionals on im E; they extend to the direct sum of the spaces of values. The non-degenerate pairing pairBK on each class and the non-zero class weights represent the extension as the preceding sum of pairings. Lifting its coefficients class by class one reaches the desired functional. The reverse inclusion is immediate from the formula. In particular the rank with unrestricted values is exactly rank E. If the equivariance law of the rows determines their values from these representatives, E is injective. In the concrete model V₀ is constructed in mtildeKGSD and the representatives of the sum come from mtildeGSD(P); applying this last conclusion requires the corresponding level compatibility. For the division columns only the data that colC really produces on the ramified source are available. It suffices that those data cover the necessary assignments modulo the annihilator of the evaluations of R; what is missing is to control how much they cover. The pointwise section does not guarantee that a lift by classes belongs to that global image.

## The representatives distinguish the rows and the terminal pairing has full rank

The level compatibility left pending in the previous paragraph can be resolved. Fix the tuple, the level P, the irreducible characters of the datum and the quotient V₀/U of the rows. We work over the number field K. For every word with interlaced weights one obtains these two statements in the rational model: (i) the evaluation of V₀ at the representatives of the period is injective; (ii) the terminal period on **all the equivariant high functions** is surjective onto the dual of any complement R of U in V₀. The proof is given because the second statement allows the stabiliser relations to be imposed simultaneously in all the classes.

For (i), let ψ be the character of the finite subgroup P.grp and let r be the representation by translations of the forms at the level mtildeKGSD. The operator goodAt that defines V₀ is the product of a central element z (sum of idempotents of characters) by e=(1/|P.grp|)∑_{a∈P.grp}ψ(a)⁻¹[a]. The change of variable in this sum gives [a]e=ψ(a)e; by centrality, every vector F of the image of r(ze) satisfies r(a)F=ψ(a)F. In RowFormsLemmas, rowTy_split decomposes the coarse level into the block of P and the level of the kernel. The transformation law of the kernel already holds because F belongs to the fine space. The law of the block is precisely the preceding character equality: the translation evaluates at the point shifted by a⁻¹, so that on shifting by a there appears ψ(a)⁻¹, as the inverted row level requires. Combined they give the complete law of mtildeGSD(P) (mem_MtildeV_iff). Any point is g·d_c for a representative d_c. If F(d_c)=0 for all of them, F(g·d_c)=ρ_fila(g)F(d_c)=0 at every point. This proves the injectivity, also on R.

For (ii), write L_u(v,y)=pairBK(brC_u v,y), where v is a high value and y a row value. The interlacing and the section of tableaux give the surjectivity of v↦L_u(v,·) onto the dual of the space of row values. Moreover L_u(ρ_alta(g)v,ρ_fila(g)y)=L_u(v,y). Indeed, for the finite part of g the two actions carry reciprocal characters (evRho and formRho). For the rational part, if A is the family of matrices of the element in the embeddings of the field, the high action uses diag(A⁻ᵗ,1), since colVal uses rhoVr and extUH fixes the last coordinate. The row action uses A, since rowVal uses rhoV. branchingProjection_act transports the first action by brStd to A⁻ᵗ when the shifts by determinant coincide, and the scalar of brC does not change this identity. The bilinear invariance pairB(A⁻ᵗ·v,A·y)=pairB(v,y) is pairingBInvariantK_holds of CoefModB. This verifies the identity of PerInv with the conventions of the period.

Given a functional on R, by (i) we extend it from the space of its evaluations. By the pointwise surjectivity we choose values v_c that represent it as ∑_c wt(c)⁻¹ L_u(v_c,F(d_c)). Let S_c be the stabiliser of the representative, finite in the class datum. We replace each v_c by

    w_c = (1/|S_c|) ∑_{g∈S_c} ρ_alta(g)v_c.

Since F(d_c) is fixed by S_c, L_u(w_c,F(d_c))=L_u(v_c,F(d_c)). Now w_c is fixed by S_c. The rule Φ(g·d_c)=ρ_alta(g)w_c is well defined, is equivariant and serves in **all** the classes at once. By definition Φ belongs to evGSD(P,u).EqFun, and its pairing PtilE restricted to R gives the initial functional. This proves (ii). No division by |S_c| was made in a residue field.

A concrete surjective map T_u:evGSD(P,u).EqFun→R* is thus identified. The effective column is its composition with colC_u on the ramified source, with the arithmetic identifications from before. For a list I, the rank defect is exactly the dimension of the rows F of R such that, for each u∈I, the functional Φ↦PtilE_u(Φ,F) vanishes on colC_u(MramV_u). Each of the individual maps that send F to a functional is injective by (ii). The proof eliminates the loss by evaluation and by stabilisers in the terminal step; what is still missing is to estimate the intersection of those annihilators for the effective images of the chain.

One can also first evaluate the chain on the whole source MfV and restrict at the end. R is kept fixed: it is not necessary that the periods on MfV descend to the quotient by U. For each list of h words, the corank on restricting to MramV increases by at most the sum of codim(MramV_u,MfV_u) over the list, by the map induced on the quotients. Under the identification of the level dimensions of i4:Llem-lvl, in expectation this costs at most hΔS₀/N dimensions. With the preceding sample size, D/κ bounded and H≤x^{C_H}b, the slope cost is O(HΔ(1+log(1+x^{C_H})))=o(b), because Δ≤f/(T_min−1), T_min≥b^{m/2}, b=exp(x^ε) and f grows at most polynomially for fixed ε. Therefore, with those dimensional inputs, it would suffice to control the rank of T_u colC_u on the complete sources in the same sample.

## The base tuple allows the auxiliary sum of the last lift to be eliminated

In the rational realisation of the trivial tuple μ₀, with the symmetric model of cstC (sharp=true), one uses oneIdx and the exceptional set Q is empty. ChainOne and KernelEquivTrivialTuple describe that specialisation; the following identities use the form of the kernels in ChKer.Shape. Only the period at μ₀ that computes the base rows is specialised. The arithmetic data that intervene in other parts of the argument are not replaced by the trivial tuple.

At the entry, startC is an isomorphism of spaces of forms over K. Indeed, oneIdx has exactly one index at each active level, the initial level at the distinguished primes is colLev(0) and, since Q is empty, a point with frames in Q has exactly one lift over each point without those frames (the empty family). The transformation law and the representation on the values are the same. Forgetting that empty decoration gives the inverse of the composition that defines startC. Therefore in this specialisation the chain can be started directly in MfV.

**Support of the coupled step.** Fix an intermediate point p at level m−1. In CplKer.comp the kernel includes a sum in Y over additive classes of vectors for that p, with summands k₂(p,Y,d) k₁(p₀,p,Y). For all the p₀ at once, k₁(p₀,p,Y) can be non-zero only in **one** of those classes. The proof identifies the class before applying the theta distributions and keeps the mass factors of the classes of p.

We use coordinate indices from zero. In PsiAt, at step i=m−2, the dimensions of origin and arrival before adding the coordinate are n'=N(m−2) and n=N(m−1); the auxiliary coordinates have lengths 1 and 0 respectively. Write k=R.k, r=k+m−1 and r_S=m/2−1. Then k<r≤a=r+(m−2)t=n'+1≤n. At each distinguished prime P, let q=qOf(P) and let f_P be the frame of p. For a representative y of Y write y'=f_P⁻¹y. The index of oneIdx carries the identity element; omPhi at (1,1) is a scalar times PhiGS, as in ookOmPhi_eq. The matrix M of entB for joinCol(x₀,y') has, for 0≤l<n,
