    M_{l,k} = q y'_l.

Indeed, the permutation fIdx of the origin sends k to n', the added column. At the arrival the permutation is the identity, and the difference of exponents scE of entB is 1. In particular the column considered is independent of x₀.

If PhiGS is non-zero, its support conditions force M_{k,k} to have residue 1. For l≥a they require the integrality of M_{l,k}/q. For r≤l<a they require residue zero because k<r. For l<r and l≠k, if r_S>0 the permutation symIdx has symIdx(k)=0 as unique minimal value, and therefore residue zero is required again. If r_S=0 (m=2), the permutation is the identity: for l>k the same inequality applies and for l<k the special clause of PhiGS with k=k+r_S applies. Thus the residue of q y' is exactly the vector e_k at each distinguished place.

To make existence and uniqueness precise, let M₀ be the intermediate lattice and M_P its localisation at P. Under UnifG of ChainCounts, q generates the local maximal ideal: its residue is zero and UnifAt gives the divisibility by q of each integral element with residue zero. For Q empty, ybSet is the quotient of the y with y∈q_P⁻¹M_P at all the distinguished places by the y with y∈M_P at those places. In the frames f_P the residue map sends y to the vectors q_P f_P⁻¹ y modulo P. It is injective by its kernel. It is also surjective: if J=(∏_P P⁻¹)M₀, the finite quotient J/M₀ is the direct sum of its localisations at those P, by the Chinese remainder theorem; at each one J_P=q_P⁻¹M_P. Any choice of the local classes is lifted in J, with no additional condition outside those places in the definition of ybSet. There exists therefore a class Y_p with all the residues e_k. This existence designates the only possible place of the support; for a given p₀ the value at Y_p may be zero.

In XiCpl1 the factors PsiAt are multiplied over the distinguished primes. For Y different from Y_p at least one is zero for every x₀, so that kerOf, which is a linear sum in XiCpl1, gives k₁=0. Thus the sum in Y is replaced exactly by k₂(p,Y_p,d) k₁(p₀,p,Y_p). Even the phase ftY of the second factor is evaluated without running over classes: for the local linear form η of XiCpl2 it is zero if some coefficient of η is not integral; otherwise it equals ψ₀(−red_P(η(e_k))), since y'≡e_k/q modulo integral vectors, epsSg=−1 and the additive character is trivial on the local integers (compatibility psi0 of ChIdx.Ok). This phase can still vary with the map x that is summed in k₂.

The computation uses the definition of the support of PhiGS in FiniteSumNonzeroLargeDepth and the permutation of LevGSym. The cardinality bound of ybSet_count by itself did not carry out this elimination. For the indicated tuple and level, the rank defect of the effective columns can no longer come from cancellations between different Y over the same p. The sums over p, over the lattice maps within each kernel and over the previous stages are still present. The rank of the resulting composition on the arithmetic rows remains without an autonomous uniform estimate.

## Evaluation of the ordinary steps at the base tuple

In the same symmetric model the whole auxiliary sum can be carried out, for each ordinary map x and at each stage i<m−2. The computation uses the definitions xx1, chArg, entB, lamGvS, PhiGS and E1at of FiniteSumNonzeroLargeDepth; ookOmPhi_eq of ChainOneOk identifies the factor in the identity frames. In those sources independence of depth and existence of a map with non-zero local kernel are already proved. Here the interest is in evaluating the kernel at a given map, even when it is zero.

We take indices from zero. Let c=m−2−i, r=k+m−1, n'=k+i(t+1), n=n'+t+1 and a=r+it=n'+c+1. At a distinguished place with prime rational uniformiser q and residue field of q elements, M is the reordered and scaled matrix of xx1. The auxiliary columns have indices s=k,…,k+c and the auxiliary rows indices p=k,…,k+c−1. In the ordinary columns the ordinary rows contain x, and the auxiliary rows are zero. We define M* by keeping the ordinary columns and putting in each auxiliary column s the vector e_s. Call y* the matrix before reordering and scaling. It is an allowed filling for A≥c+1 and B≥1: in the first c auxiliary columns the diagonal ones are already fixed; in the last one the ordinary coefficient of physical row k is taken equal to q^{−(c+1)}. The other free coefficients are taken to be zero. In particular chArg(y*)=0.

**Formula.** Let D be the sum, over the free coefficients, of their scaling exponents d, and let H be the number of these coefficients whose scaled residue is prescribed by the support. Then, for any x,

    E1at(i,P,1,1)(x) = ookKap(i,P) q^{D−H} PhiGS(y*).

The integers D,H depend only on the dimensions and on the permutation of the support. For the purpose of counting H each entry forced to have residue 0 or 1 is counted only once, even if it intervenes in several conditions. The entries below the first a rows have residue zero.

**Proof.** The integrality, the residue conditions of the diagonal and the zeros outside the blocks are checked entry by entry. Hence the tests on the ordinary columns are independent of the free coefficients. In the initial block of order r the matrix of residues is triangular with diagonal one after permuting rows and columns simultaneously by symIdx. In the remaining it rows of the initial square matrix of order a, the groups of size t are in block triangular form, with zeros to the left of the first group. The determinant modulo P is therefore the product of the determinants of those i diagonal blocks. All are in ordinary columns, since k+c<r. This proves that both the multiplicative characters and the invertibility test of the determinant are independent of the filling that satisfies the support tests.

The additive trace of lamGvS sums the entries (p,p+1) up to r−1, omitting the link (k−1,k). The variable entries of the trace are precisely M_{k+v,k+v+1}, for 0≤v<c. Each is q times the coefficient of physical row n+v and physical column n'+v+1 of xx1: the difference of exponents between these two positions is one. On summing, the variable part of the trace is exactly q chArg. On the support it is integral, and the compatibility ψ(u/q)=ψ₀(red u) for integral u cancels its character with ψ(−chArg). The other entries of the trace are fixed, including those that are zero. Each admissible filling therefore contributes the same value as y*. If an ordinary condition fails, or a diagonal block is singular, all contribute zero.

The counting factor remains. In the auxiliary column s=k+v, a free ordinary row has exponent d=v+1 and a free auxiliary row k+w has d=v−w with w<v. Thus 1≤d≤c+1. Writing the free variable as z/q^A with 0≤z<q^{A+B}, the integrality of its scaled version is equivalent to q^{A−d}∣z. There are q^{B+d} possibilities and exactly q^{B+d−1} for each prescribed residue. These choices are independent by the entry-by-entry tests. The original weight contributes q^{−B} per free variable. Multiplying gives q^{D−H}, as claimed.

**Consequence for the ordinary map.** If the local factor is non-zero, x is integral. M* reduced has non-zero initial determinant of order a. On expanding along its c+1 canonical columns one obtains the ordinary minor of x in the first n'+1 physical rows, deleting row k and keeping all its columns. Its determinant is a unit. The following physical rows are zero modulo P. Thus, within the first n'+1 coordinates, the reduced image of x and the line of the vector e_k are complementary. The statement refers to the map x in the frames of the kernel. A sum of maps of full rank with characters or coefficients of periods may lose rank; this formula does not estimate that loss on the arithmetic rows.

## Which component of the theta distributions reaches the period

Keep the base tuple and the ordinary stages. At a distinguished place P of residue field F_q, for 1≤i<m−2 call Z_ℓ the reduction of the diagonal block of size t in the rows and columns r+ℓt,…,r+(ℓ+1)t−1 of the matrix M* of step i, for 0≤ℓ<i. These blocks contain only entries of the ordinary map. In the preceding formula their whole effect on the local factor is exactly

    ∏_{ℓ<i} h_{ℓi}(Z_ℓ),     h_{ℓi}(Z)=η_{ℓi}(det Z) if Z is invertible, and 0 otherwise,
    η_{ℓi}=χ_ℓ χ_i⁻¹.

Indeed, the denominator uses χ_i of the product of the determinants and the numerator uses χ_ℓ on the block ℓ. The additive trace ends in the first r coordinates. The prescribed zeros between blocks impose no condition inside the Z_ℓ. The injectivity of j↦χ_j at each distinguished place (Chars.Ok.chi_inj of AbelianHostFieldConditions, with chiP of FiniteSumNonzeroLargeDepth) makes each η_{ℓi} non-trivial. This is a specific use of the distinction between the characters of the datum.

One obtains an identity for the **complete period** in the data of the construction. Fix word, row and input vector. Expand the finite sums over classes and over maps of kerOf in the composition colC, and lastly the pairing of the row. In each term that has passed the integrality and support requirements we choose the coordinates in the frames of that term. Discard the terms with some singular Z_ℓ. Let J be the set of the preceding triples (P,i,ℓ) and let D=∏_{j∈J} F_{q_j}^×. For d∈D, sum in A(d) all the terms with vector of determinants d, removing from each one only the factors η_j(d_j). An empty fibre contributes zero. In A there remain the theta distributions, the additive phases, the weights of the classes, the level conditions, the final steps and the pairing. By multilinearity the period is exactly

    B = ∑_{d∈D} η(d) A(d),             η(d)=∏_{j∈J} η_j(d_j).

This grouping holds also if the determinants of the rational maps at different places are correlated. It does not require realising by a global map an independent change of its residues.

Let E_j be the average in the coordinate j of a table on D. By orthogonality of characters, in this formula A can be replaced by (∏_j (I−E_j))A. In particular, every table that is a sum of functions each independent of at least one of the coordinates contributes **exactly zero**. For g distinguished places there are g(m−3)(m−2)/2 coordinates when m≥4. Already at m=4 one appears per place. Therefore a model that replaces the fibres of the period by their mean masses, even if it computes them exactly in all the proper subfamilies of determinants, loses precisely the coefficient needed. This does not rule out that a product of functions with dependence on all the coordinates reproduces it.

The loss of information persists for non-negative tables. If J is not empty, the real tables A₀(d)=1 and A₁(d)=1+Re(η(d)⁻¹) have identical sum in any coordinate and, hence, all the same proper marginals. The first gives B=0. In the second B=(|D|+∑_d η(d)²)/2, which equals |D| or |D|/2 according as η² is trivial or not. It is a proof of insufficiency of those data, not a claim that the theta distributions realise both tables.

The same selection admits an additive description before grouping by determinant. For a block, fix a complex embedding and a non-trivial additive character ψ. The unnormalised transform of h, with the pairing tr(YZ), equals zero if Y is singular and
η(det Y)⁻¹ C if Y is invertible, with |C|=q^{t²/2}. To see this, if Y is singular, choose g acting as the identity on a complement of ker Y and rescaling a line of the kernel in such a way that η(det g)≠1. Since Yg=Y, changing Z to gZ in the sum forces its vanishing. For Y invertible we replace Z by Y⁻¹Z. By Parseval the sum of squares of moduli is q^{t²}|GL_t(F_q)|, which fixes |C|. This is a case of the matrix Gauss sums studied by [Li and Hu](https://arxiv.org/abs/1105.4513). On grouping first by the residual matrices, giving value zero to the empty fibres, finite inversion shows that only their frequencies of full rank in **all** the blocks can contribute. The preceding multiplicative formula selects a specific coefficient even within that part.

The computation identifies an obstruction to the use of averages without characters. It does not give a lower bound for the rank of the matrix of those coefficients as row and column vary. For the sampling route one needs to control that rank on concatenating words in the same target.

## A normal form for the blocks of the whole trajectory

There is more structure in the concrete characters of the two generating primes. At the base tuple one can normalise **the complete matrices of the diagonal blocks of all the stages**, and keep the composite theta contribution. Only one block per generating rational prime needs to be excepted. This makes precise what multiplicative information was needed jointly in the preceding table.

Let s be a stage between 0 and m. At a distinguished place P above g, with g one of the two generating primes, the augmented coordinates of the source of stage s<m have dimension r+st. At the final level we use the projected coordinates of PhiTop, of dimension r+mt. On each arrow i→i+1 take the residual matrix of the term in the support, with the canonical filling in the ordinary stages. For i=m−2 one takes the matrix of PsiAt on joinCol with the class already fixed, and for i=m−1 the matrix of PhiTop after the final projection. The three definitions use PhiGS with j=i+1. Hence, on each arrow, the initial matrix of order r is triangular after symIdx with diagonal one and, below it, there appear i invertible diagonal blocks Z_{P,i,ℓ}, 0≤ℓ<i, of order t. This is the square part of order r+it. There are zeros below the diagonal of blocks and below that square part. In particular the determinant factors of the last two arrows obey the same formula as in the ordinary ones.

The residue field at P is identified canonically with F_g because g splits completely. The clause chi_om of Chars.Ok gives an injection ω_g:F_g^×→K^× common to all the P of the group, with χ_i(z)=ω_g(z)^{2(i+1)}. Therefore, for **all** the arrows, the product of determinant factors over the P of this group is ω_g(D_g)⁻², where

    D_P = ∏_{i=1}^{m−1} ∏_{ℓ<i} det(Z_{P,i,ℓ})^{i−ℓ},       D_g = ∏_{P over g in the datum} D_P.

The additive phases and the theta operators are kept separately. Already in the original coordinates the table of summands can be grouped by the pair (D_{g₀},D_{g₁}) and evaluated with these two characters. There are at most (g₀−1)(g₁−1)/4 character labels if each D_g is identified with −D_g, since both primes are odd. This bound counts possible values of the labels, not the cost of obtaining their fibres.

**Change of frames.** At each internal vertex s, choose matrices G_{P,s,ℓ} invertible in F_g for ℓ<s. In the ordinary coordinates one lifts diag(I_{k+s},G_{P,s,0},…,G_{P,s,s−1}) to an integral local automorphism with integral inverse. It suffices to lift each block entry by entry to rational integers, since its determinant modulo P is a unit. At the two ends the change is the identity. With the auxiliary coordinates inserted, this leaves the first r coordinates fixed and acts by the G on the remaining ones. In the matrices of a trajectory the substitution of the frames on the right gives

    Z'_{P,i,ℓ} = G_{P,i+1,ℓ}⁻¹ Z_{P,i,ℓ} G_{P,i,ℓ}.

The support tests are preserved by the block triangular structure. The entries used in the additive trace belong to the first r coordinates and do not change. The canonical filling still has its same auxiliary columns. In the coupled step the physical vector e_k is left fixed and, in ftY, the covariable and the vector are transformed by G and G⁻¹, so that its evaluation and the integrality requirement are preserved. In this computation the rational maps, the points, the class weights and the representatives of the quotients are the original ones. Local factors are evaluated in different auxiliary frames within the summand. The section used to enumerate the classes is not replaced by that procedure.

Write b_{P,s}=∏_{ℓ<s}det G_{P,s,ℓ}. In the composition of the theta operators of the term, changing the frame of s multiplies by (χ_s χ_{s−1}⁻¹)(b_{P,s})=ω_g(b_{P,s})². Indeed, ThDist.Ok.det uses rhoT on the incoming arrow, which at P is χ_{s−1}⁻¹, and rhoS on the outgoing one, which is χ_s. The same holds when the outgoing one is top, by ChTh.Ok.top. They are scalars in K, so they come out of the composition and of the final pairing. This law is also obtained directly for the distribution of the construction in KernelLocalFactors/ThDistC, thDistC_det. In the determinant part, by the formula for Z', D'_P/D_P=∏_{s=1}^{m−1} b_{P,s}: each G_{P,s,ℓ} appears with exponent (s−ℓ)−(s−1−ℓ)=1, counting the second exponent as zero in a newly created block. The two responses to the change of frames are reciprocal.

**Choice of frames.** In each group over g choose a place P₀. Prescribe Z'=I on all the arrows and places except at (P₀,m−1,m−2), where we put diag(D_g,1,…,1). These prescriptions are always realised: starting from G_{P,m,ℓ}=I we solve backwards G_{P,i,ℓ}=Z_{P,i,ℓ}⁻¹ G_{P,i+1,ℓ} Z'_{P,i,ℓ} for ℓ<i. There is no block to normalise at the vertex 0. Since the exceptional block has exponent one, ∏_{P over g}D'_P=D_g. Thus ∏_{P over g,s} b_{P,s}=1. The theta composition in the chosen frames is **equal** to the original one, even before multiplying by the explicit characters. These are now read only in the two exceptional blocks. The construction holds for t≥1 and m≥2.

The choice of frames depends on the trajectory. It does not identify the theta operators of two different trajectories with the same D_g. The rational maps, the additive phases and the periods remain in the sums of each label. An estimate of the rank of the combination of those sums, uniform as the datum varies, is still necessary.

The additive phase of the same arrows can also be read at the end. Let U_i be the initial block of order r of the residual matrix and let L(U)=∑_{0≤p<r−1, p≠k−1} U_{p,p+1}. For matrices in the support one has L(UV)=L(U)+L(V). In the order symIdx, the indices p,p+1 of the sum are neighbours except, if r_S>0, the pair (k+r_S−1,k+r_S). In an entry between neighbours the product contributes only the two original entries because the diagonals are one. For the exceptional pair the possible intermediate indices in that order are the physical indices h<k, and V_{h,k+r_S}=0 by the additional clause of PhiGS. The product preserves that clause: in (UV)_{h,k+r_S}, h<k, the indices between h and k+r_S in the permuted order still belong to the first physical group h'<k, and the end term U_{h,k+r_S} is also zero. This allows iteration without additional hypothesis, including the case r_S=0. Hence the total phase of the traces at P is ψ₀(L(U_{m−1}⋯U₀)). In the ordinary stages the canonical filling was used, whose phase chArg is zero.

In the last step ftY has to be included. With the class Y_p fixed, this contributes ψ₀(−red_P(η_P(e_k))) under the integrality requirement on the form η_P of XiCpl2. Hence for the group over g it suffices to take the residual sum

    A_g = ∑_{P over g in the datum} [ L(U_{P,m−1}⋯U_{P,0}) − red_P(η_P(e_k)) ]   in F_g.

The compatibility characterAgreesOnPlaces gives the same additive character ψ_g at all those P. On each admitted trajectory, the explicit scalar of characters at the distinguished places is thus ∏_{g=g₀,g₁} ψ_g(A_g)ω_g(D_g)⁻², apart from the fixed normalisations of the kernels. The theta distribution and the pairing remain operators and values of the construction. The reduction to two pairs of residues allows grouping without losing any of these phases.

## The level prime reads only the composition of the rational maps

Fix the word u, the base tuple and a row point d. Let S_T be the set of distinguished level places of u, over the rational prime T_u. It is disjoint from the places over the generating primes g₀,g₁. At each vertex of the chain, forget for a moment only the level linear forms f_{s,P}, P∈S_T. Keep the lattice N_s, all the decorations at the generating primes and, at the end, the point tilQ(d) **with** its prescribed linear form. This forgetting is used to carry out a finite sum, without changing the spaces of forms of the period.

**Elimination formula.** On a trajectory of rational maps x_i, 0≤i<m, choose representatives of the quotients of kerOf. For P∈S_T, write V_{s,P}=N_{s,P}/PN_{s,P}. Each x_i induces a residual map X_{i,P}:V_{i,P}→V_{i+1,P}: the authorised denominator cA has only factors g₀,g₁. The change of representative is a multiple of cB, divisible by T_u, and therefore does not alter X_{i,P}. Let λ_P be the linear form of tilQ(d) on V_{m,P}. In the last map one uses x_{m−1} integral, including the last coordinate. Then the only value of the initial decoration that can intervene is

    v_P = λ_P X_{m−1,P} X_{m−2,P} ⋯ X_{0,P}.

If some v_P is zero, the trajectory does not contribute. Otherwise, the contribution evaluates the input form at the initial decoration (v_P)_{P∈S_T}. No sum over choices of intermediate f_{s,P} remains.

Indeed, a level decoration on a fixed lattice is equivalent to a **non-zero** linear form on V_{s,P}. The additivity and the law of scalars of Deco.Ok give the factorisation through P and the residual linearity. Localisation does not change N_s/PN_s, so that f_ne is equivalent to the residual form not being zero. Outside the localised lattice its value is prescribed equal to zero. On the arrow i, phiT imposes literally f_{i,P}=f_{i+1,P} X_{i,P}. From λ_P all the forms are thus prescribed. If the first is non-zero, all its predecessors in the backward computation are also non-zero. This applies in XiEntry and on the two arrows of XiCpl1 and XiCpl2. The remaining parts of the kernels, including the domain of kerOf, the representatives for its sum, the theta distributions and the class Y, depend on the lattices and on the frames at the generating primes, independently of f_{s,P}. The condition that defines liftFr there employs only the lattice and its decoration be.

**Class weights.** For each orbit of points after the forgetting, let H be the stabiliser of a representative and Ω the set of its possible decorations at S_T. The fine orbits over it are identified with H\Ω. If H_f fixes f, the fine weight in the sum over classes is 1/|H_f|. Here H is finite: Ω is finite and non-empty and the fine stabilisers are finite by the class datum; the index of H_f is the size of its orbit. For a contribution that is invariant after summing the maps of the arrows, the orbit-stabiliser identity gives

    ∑_{[f]∈H\Ω} C(f)/|H_f| = (1/|H|) ∑_{f∈Ω} C(f).

It applies simultaneously at the summed vertices, including the initial one if its class is summed. The equivariance of the kernels cancels the actions at the internal vertices, and the input form satisfies the transformation law at the initial one. The weight of the final row remains that of the original period. Afterwards the kernels are expanded in finite sums and the order is exchanged with the sum over Ω. This justifies carrying out the elimination for each individual x_i although an individual summand is not invariant under H. Each orbit after the forgetting carries its weight 1/|H| only once. The identities of weights and the independence of the section are wt_eq and opOf_indep in GSetLemmas. The definitions of the decorations are those of StdLatUnimodular; those of the three kernels are those of FiniteSumNonzeroLargeDepth.

**Exact test on the ramified source.** For P∈S_T choose a generator a_P of the cyclic group of the multiplicative residue field. On the complete source denote by R_P the action that on evaluating a form replaces f_P by a_P f_P. Over K of characteristic zero,

    MramV_u = image of ∏_{P∈S_T}(I−R_P).

By definition MramV_u is the intersection of the kernels of the sums of the powers of R_P. If e_P is that sum divided by the order of the group, it projects to the invariants. The image of I−R_P is ker e_P: it is contained in it and both have the same dimension by rank and nullity. The operators commute and I−R_P is invertible on ker e_P, which proves the formula on restricting to the intersection. This holds over K even when the action on the classes has stabilisers. The actions and the space are diaG_apply in AtTLemmas and ColRamV in ScaledRamifiedLattice.

Thus the ramified columns are obtained by applying the period to inputs of the complete source and replacing, on each admissible trajectory, its value φ(v) by ∑_{J⊆S_T}(−1)^{|J|}φ(v^{J}), where v^J_P=a_P v_P for P∈J and v^J_P=v_P at the other places. For a fixed origin its other decorations in the argument of φ are understood. The test uses at most 2^{|S_T|} evaluations per trajectory without running over the (T_u−1)^{|S_T|} scalars or imposing conditions of descent to the quotient on the complete source. Unlike the cost estimated by codimension, the image of these columns is exactly the ramified image over K.

For the rank a concrete question remains: on summing with the theta weights and the phases of g₀,g₁, do those differences still distinguish enough rows? The residual composition fixes their arguments and eliminates the intermediate decorations from the computation. It does not determine the weights of the trajectories that end in the same argument.

**Which part of the theta can contribute at the level prime.** The preceding elimination allows each theta distribution, on each arrow and each P∈S_T, to be projected exactly onto the additive frequencies of **rank one** in the matrix variable X_{i,P}. The period on the whole ramified source is preserved. In particular, replacing one of these distributions by its average in that variable annihilates the period.

To justify the use of Fourier, in the sum of maps of kerOf fix the components of the class at the other places. The free component at P runs uniformly over the whole of Hom(V_{i,P},V_{i+1,P}). Indeed, homAt is the module of homomorphisms between the two lattices over the semilocal ring of integers at the places of Sall: the membership condition is checked in each localisation. It is obtained by localising the module Hom of the global lattices, since these are finite projective over the ring of integers. The quotient of divLat(cA) by mulLat(cB), after multiplying by cA, is that of that module by cA cB. By the Chinese remainder theorem it is the product of the local quotients. At P, cA is a unit and cB has valuation one by the complete splitting of T_u, which gives the arbitrary residual matrix. In the local coordinates the class is identified with the reduction of the original map, undoing the unit factor cA. The distributions are considered on this product: ThDist.Ok.cong makes them well defined for any choice of representatives.

Fix now the other maps of the trajectory and the other local components. Write X=X_{i,P}, Y=X_{i−1,P}⋯X_{0,P} and l=λ_P X_{m−1,P}⋯X_{i+1,P}, with the identity for an empty product. Outside the theta distribution of the arrow itself, the dependence on X is solely through the input evaluation at lXY. It is extended by zero when the argument is zero. For a ramified input form, on each orbit of non-zero scalars its sum is zero by NmOp. Therefore the mean of this function of X is zero: if l≠0, the linear map X↦lXY covers its image with equal fibres and that image is a subspace stable under scalars. If l=0 the function is identically zero. This holds component by component of the input vector.

Under the pairing tr(FX), the frequencies of the pullback by X↦lXY belong to the image of the dual map, formed by matrices (Yv)l, with v in V_{0,P}. This is seen by summing the character over the translations of the kernel of the map. They are of rank at most one and the zero frequency has just been eliminated. On pairing with the theta table only its opposite frequencies enter, also of rank one. This argument works after composing with all the remaining linear operators of the term. The projections can be applied successively on all the arrows and places: replacing a table by its projection in its own variable maintains its independence of the variables of the other arrows, and at different places of a table the transforms commute. One can work after a complex embedding; the rank of the matrices of the period in characteristic zero does not change under this extension.

The filter is different at the two types of primes. At g₀,g₁ the determinant character selected frequencies of full rank in the t by t blocks. At P over T_u it is the linear decoration of the end, together with the ramification of the source, that forces rank exactly one. This second conclusion refers to the table of the effective theta operators in the quotient of kerOf, even after summing the trajectories. Knowing its total mass at that place controls none of the frequencies that intervene.

## The level frequencies before taking the theta derivatives

Fix an arrow of dimensions a,b, its two lattices, its frames and the other local classes of the quotient of kerOf. Let P be a level place, q=T_u. In the complex realisation of this arrow, for each frequency F on Hom(V_{i,P},V_{i+1,P}) one obtains an **entire function** Θ_F before taking the derivatives that give the table of the operator. In an admitted datum with non-zero initial section, all these functions are orthogonal and have the same positive norm when the cell of the other places is non-empty. In particular this holds for the (q^a−1)(q^b−1)/(q−1) frequencies of rank one. The statement is per arrow and per pair of lattices. We make precise both the norm and the evaluation that the period still requires.

Let c=cA cB and write A,B,Λ for the complex lattices AC, BC, LamC of the model of XiIndCClass, and L=A∩B. We use the data of self-duality, semicharacters and commensurable lattices of SplitHyp. Here B is a lattice, distinct from the period symbol of the previous sections. The finite group C=A/L has dual B/L through

    [y,t] = exp(−2πi Im H(t,y)).

Indeed, an element of A that annihilates B belongs to the dual of B, which is B, and similarly on the other side. Passing to the two finite quotients one obtains injections of each into the dual of the other, hence they are isomorphisms. The deltas of the classes of C constitute a basis of the space of functions on C.

For the initial theta section e of the pair of lattices, the linear map I from the functions on C to the entire sections is xiL with volume 1. The laws XiTranslate and XiModulate intertwine it with translation in A and multiplication by [y,t] for y∈B. In the target the respective actions are α(x)U(x), where U(x)h(z)=exp(−πH(z,x)−πH(x,x)/2)h(z+x). These laws come from the finite sums of Alpoge's model (twist_translate and twist_modulate in AlternatingFormStandardHermitian). By XiTieL, XiTieS and XiVacuum, I takes the indicator of (Λ∩A+L)/L to e: its restriction to the representatives of (Λ∩A)/L in the small sum is constant one. Here L⊆Λ in the concrete model, as is seen in each localisation from halfAO and halfBO. Therefore I is non-zero whenever e is. For the admitted lattices the construction obtains e from thZero by two operators Iop and a non-zero scalar (LateralTheory, eNat; thZero_ne_zero and thMoved_ne_zero in IsAdmissibleFlagPairOfUnitriangularLeftAutomorph). These are inputs on the functions of one arrow, prior to any estimate of periods.

Give the sections the Hermitian norm obtained by integrating |h(z)|² exp(−πH(z,z)) over the torus by L, with one and the same measure. The density is periodic because the sections have the same semicharacter on L. This is also seen on the deltas: for x∈L the character [x,t] is identically one, so that α(x)U(x) fixes their images. For x in A or B the translations are unitary by change of variable in that integral and |α(x)|=1. By intertwining, I(δ_t) is an eigenvector for each y∈B with eigenvalue [y,t]. Distinct deltas have distinct characters by the perfect duality. Their images are orthogonal, and the translations of A show that all have the same norm. Since I is not zero, this norm is positive. In particular, for any table Φ on C one has exactly

    ||I(Φ)||² = c_e ∑_{t∈C}|Φ(t)|²,       c_e = ||e||² / |(Λ∩A)/L|.

To apply it to the table of kerOf, use the reading ThDatCut of ThDatRead and the realisation by derivatives unfTh_kerOf of RecChainUnfOne. The lattice of periods perO localised at P is Hom(N_{i,P},N_{i+1,P}) by AAUnit. The places of Sall and their conjugates are disjoint. At each place of Sall, C is described by c⁻¹perO_P/c perO_P, and at the conjugate place there is no component of C, because there A is contained in B. At P the cut of kerOf selects the subgroup of integral matrices modulo P, since v_P(cA)=0 and v_P(cB)=1. Fixing the other classes of kerOf lifts to a cell: a product of this subgroup by a fixed finite set at the other places. This is deduced from the Chinese remainder theorem; at the other places a single class of the level cB may have several lifts to the level c. Let Φ₀ be its indicator. The factors constant on this cell can be taken out of the sum. With ψ a non-trivial additive character on F_q, let Φ_F(t)=Φ₀(t)ψ(tr(FX)), where X is its residual matrix at P. Then Θ_F=I(Φ_F), with the convention of unnormalised transform and positive sign. The tables Φ_F are orthogonal by additive orthogonality on the subgroup. The preceding isometry gives

    ⟨Θ_F,Θ_{F'}⟩ = c_e |support Φ₀| · 1_{F=F'}.

These functions can also be obtained by means of **a single section without character**. The character ψ(tr(FX)) of the integral subgroup at P extends to C: in a finite abelian group a character is extended to an additional generator by choosing a root of the value already prescribed at its first multiple that belongs to the subgroup. The trivial character is taken at the other places. By duality there exists a class y_F∈B/L that realises the extension. Different extensions give the same product on Φ₀. The modulation law gives the exact formula

    Θ_F(z) = α(y_F) exp(−πH(z,y_F)−πH(y_F,y_F)/2) Θ₀(z+y_F).

In particular, the table of the arrow that does intervene in the preceding proof is computed by taking the prescribed derivatives of this expression at z=0, with the scales cD and the common scalar of unfTh_kerOf. They are derivatives of Θ₀ at y_F, with the terms of lower orders that the exponential produces. The representative y_F need not have order q in B/L: on extending a character of pZ/p²Z to the group Z/p²Z a root of order p² may already be necessary.

The orthogonality controls exactly the functions **before** those derivatives. For an operator J of evaluation of derivatives, the matrix that matters afterwards has entries ⟨JΘ_F,JΘ_{F'}⟩. The integral norm computed does not by itself give a floor for that matrix nor for the composition between classes and words. This is a concrete limitation of trying to prove the rank by appealing only to orthogonality of characters.

**A rank test that is applied after the derivatives.** For a word and a class of origin with the level data forgotten, let E₀ be the space of values of the polynomial at the origin. For P∈S_T put V_P=V_{0,P}. On the functions on V_P^* we use the orthogonal projection Π_P that subtracts the mean under F_q^×. The projected functions are automatically zero at the zero covariable. Given w_P∈V_P, the projection of the function b↦ψ(bw_P) equals k_P(bw_P), where

    k_P(0)=0,                  k_P(t)=ψ(t)+1/(q−1) for t≠0.

Indeed, at t≠0 the sum of ψ over its scalar orbit equals −1. In the product of places the projection is the product of the Π_P. Choose any Hermitian product on E₀ invariant under the finite stabiliser H of the coarse class, averaging it if necessary. The functions

    b ↦ v ∏_P k_P(b_P w_P),       w_P∈V_P, v in an orthonormal basis of E₀,

projected moreover by the equivariance average of H, form a spanning system of the whole ramified source in the class. With the counting product on the covariables, each vector of the system being divided by (∏_P q^{dim V_P})^{1/2}, they form a Parseval frame: the normalised additive characters before projecting form an orthonormal basis, and the two orthogonal projections commute because H transports the covariables linearly. The indices with some w_P=0 can be omitted. Distinct classes give direct summands. This metric is used solely for the finite Gram identity.

In particular, applying the real period of the construction to this normalised system one obtains a matrix L whose Gram LL* is that of the ramified map with that metric. This preserves the rank exactly also on concatenating words in one and the same target. On evaluating each vector, on the trajectory of the preceding paragraph its scalar argument is λ_P X_{m−1,P}⋯X_{0,P}w_P. Only trajectories where that scalar is non-zero at all the places are used for the corresponding evaluation, with the transports of w_P and v in the summands of the average by H. The theta coefficients here have already passed through the required derivatives. The Gram equality expresses the values that would have to be bounded in order to control the rank; the integral orthogonality of the sections does not by itself estimate LL*.

## Effective loss on evaluating the derivatives at the standard lattices

On an arrow between the two standard lattices, there are whole subfamilies of frequencies of rank one for which a large drop of the rank after taking the derivatives can be **proved**. We use the size bounds of the working class of the annex and the effective weights of the arrow, without imposing the global sign. Denote by n and n' its dimensions of origin and arrival, by q=T_u the level prime and by f both the number of its selected places and that of embeddings in the CM type. Use the standard global bases to describe the residues. A non-empty cell at the other places is fixed as in the preceding paragraph.

Fix an origin index α. At each P let the frequency vary among all the matrices F=e_α l_P of size n by n', where l_P runs over the non-zero covectors of F_q^{n'}. It is a family of (q^{n'}−1)^f orthogonal theta sections. One can also fix a coordinate of the arrival end and let the vectors of the origin vary. In the first case let s=n' and in the second s=n. Let w_τ be the width of the partition of the arrow after the shift that jetOp uses: if λ is the normalised partition of the source and ν=nuLast, then w_τ=λ_{1,τ}+ν_τ. The rank over C of the family of **value operators** obtained on evaluating the derivatives satisfies

    rank ≤ ∏_τ binom(w_τ+s,s),
    rank / (q^s−1)^f ≤ ((1+max_τ w_τ)/(q−1))^{sf}.

The second inequality uses binom(w+s,s)≤(w+1)^s, counting exponents coordinate by coordinate, and q^s−1≥(q−1)^s.

**Separation of variables in this cell.** For standard lattices, homO is the module of all the integer matrices, and perO is the product of copies of the same ideal, one per entry. LamC, AC and BC are thus products by matrix entries. Each entry keeps its f archimedean coordinates jointly. HV is an orthogonal sum by entries and alV is the exponential of the corresponding quadratic form, so that the translation factors and the semicharacters are products. The starting section is proportional to tensPow(thMM): the two moves Iop of thMoved are from a lattice to itself and fix the section; the identity is iopSelf in CharacterFactorUnitaryAction. In the standard basis, fixing classes of matrices at the other places prescribes congruences entry by entry. Therefore the tables of the cell and their modulation by ψ(tr(FX)) are products. Choosing Cartesian representatives in the sums that define xiL and xiS, and distributing the finite sums, the resulting section is also a product. The independence of the representatives allows that choice. These steps use the definitions of XiIndCClass and LateralTheory.

For F=e_α l_P, the character changes only the s coefficients of the column α of X at each P. In the analytic section, grouping the factors of that column, there remains a variable function H of s coordinates per embedding multiplied by a fixed function of all the other coordinates. One has the same conclusion by rows in the other case. The orthogonality before taking derivatives is obtained by applying the isometry of the preceding paragraph at all the P: the tables of two distinct labels are orthogonal at at least one place.

**Which orders are evaluated.** At each embedding the polynomial symbol of the step is constructed by substituting DᵗZ in the input polynomial multiplied by det^ν, as in subD and jetMap of ThDistC. In a vector of the basis of tableaux there intervenes a product of w_τ minors, including the ν determinants. Each minor of DᵗZ is of degree at most one in any complete column of D: that column intervenes in a single row of DᵗZ. It is also of degree at most one in any complete row of D: by Cauchy–Binet each summand uses that index at most once. Thus the total order of the derivative on the s variable entries is at most w_τ at τ, even though derivatives of much higher order are used in other entries. Linearity gives this bound for the whole source. The product of H by the fixed factor implies that each operator of the family is a combination of fixed matrices with coefficients the derivatives of H of those multi-orders at zero. There are at most ∏_τ binom(w_τ+s,s) coefficients. The frame scalars and the common weight projections preserve the bound.

**Size in the parameters of the annex.** With indices of the chain from i=0, lamTrue_natW of ColInst identifies w_τ with the first part of the normalised partition of the arrival. For j=i+1≥1, the first entry of wtQ is u₀−(k−1)/2 and the last is −wG(e_j,j). Subtracting them eliminates the common shift. Therefore w_τ≤u₀+e_j, using wG(e_j,j)=e_j−k/2−(2j−1)(t+1)/2. In the notation of the annex u₀ is the first entry (also written u₁ when the index starts at one). The clauses (L), (P) of «The records of the working class» and its bound u₁≤b³ give e_j≤b²+η≤b²+b for large x, because tη≤w″/x and w″≤b. With L=log b one gets 1+max w_τ≤3b³ eventually, uniformly in the word and in the arrow. By TuSize, q≥b^{m/2} and m tends to infinity. The fraction of dimensions that remains in any of these families is at most (3b³/(b^{m/2}−1))^{sf} and tends to zero. This is the dimension of the image divided by the number of independent sections before evaluating. It does not imply that an individual frequency has zero operator.

An arbitrary rotation of the residual direction does not preserve the factorisation used. In fact every integral isometry of the standard Hermitian lattice is monomial. In a unitary column (a_j), on averaging ∑_j |τ(a_j)|²=1 over the CM type, each non-zero algebraic integer contributes at least one by the arithmetic-geometric mean and |Norm_{E/Q}(a_j)|≥1. There is therefore a single non-zero entry per column and, by invertibility, in distinct rows. This symmetry only permutes the coordinate directions. The result rules out using the independence of **all** the frequencies as an intermediate lemma even for a standard arrow. The rank that the slope inequality requires refers to the rows of periods after combining places, classes and words; it still requires its own estimate.

**Cost of omitting the coordinate directions at the origin of the chain.** There is a way of getting round this obstruction in the rank test at the origin. In each coarse class choose arbitrary bases of V_P and keep in the Parseval frame only the tuples w that have **all their coordinates non-zero at all the P**. We claim only that a system with small loss of rank remains; on removing vectors the exact Parseval identity for the original Gram ceases to apply.

For the word u write d⁰_u=dim MfV_u and suppose the freeness of the stabilisers of initial fine class of i4:lem-lowest. The codimension of the system kept in the ramified source is at most

    δ_u d⁰_u,     δ_u = 1−[(q−1)^k/(q^k−1)]^f < fk/q       (k>1).

Indeed, if Ω is the product of the non-zero parts of the spaces of residual vectors, the fraction discarded is δ_u, by counting. The upper bound is obtained by adding the probabilities (q^{k−1}−1)/(q^k−1)<1/q that a coordinate is zero. Let H be the stabiliser of the coarse class. It acts freely on the tuples of non-zero covectors: their stabilisers are precisely those of fine class. It also acts freely on Ω. If an element fixes at each P some non-zero vector, its matrix minus the identity is singular at each P. The dual action also fixes, at each P, a non-zero covector. The freeness on the tuples of covectors then forces the element to be the identity.

The vectors of the frame associated with one and the same orbit of w span the same subspace as the vector of the module of values E₀ varies: the change of w to h·w is absorbed by the invertible action on E₀ and the average by H. The ramified projection commutes with that action. Thus only the **entirely** discarded orbits can be lost, each of cost at most dim E₀. They are at most δ_u|Ω|/|H|, even if the bases chosen are not H-invariant. The dimension of the complete source in that class is (dim E₀)|Ω|/|H| by the freeness on the covectors. Summing over classes gives the formula.

In the uniform sample of h words, with S₀=∑d⁰_u and N_pal possible words, the additional loss of expected rank is bounded by h S₀ max δ_u/N_pal. For the sample size and the parameters already deduced above, hS₀/(N_pal κ)=O(1+log(1+x^{C_H})) and H≤x^{C_H}b. The additional cost in the slope inequality is therefore o(b), since q≥b^{m/2} and f,k are polynomial in x. It thus suffices to look for a rank bound at the origin using only the dense directions in any chosen basis. The estimate is of the cost of removing generators at the origin; it gives no licence to suppress simultaneously all the trajectories that pass through a sparse direction at some interior vertex.

## A dense direction in which the derivatives lose rank

Take the first ordinary arrow between standard lattices, at the base tuple, with identity frames. Let k and s be its source and target dimensions, q the level prime and M=g₀g₁. Take an integer vector v of length k with non-zero reduction modulo q and put D=∑vᵢ². At each place P above q considered in the construction we fix that same direction and vary independently the non-zero covector l_P of length s. The frequency is v l_P. One may use the full ordinary table at the primes g, provided it is not identically zero, or restrict it to a non-zero residue class of its support modulo those primes. In both cases, for N=(qˢ−1)ᶠ, the N sections are linearly independent before evaluation. For their operators after the derivatives one has

    rank / N ≤ [D M² (1+max w_τ)/(q−1)]^(fs).

Here w_τ is the width of the symbol of the arrow defined above. In particular v=(1,…,1) has all its coordinates non-zero and, nevertheless, the rank fraction tends to zero. The statement concerns the joint independence of the labels l_P in this fixed pair of lattices.

**Common periods.** Let Φ₀ be the table without character at the places of q and Θ₀=I(Φ₀). At the primes g the formula of E1at gives integral support and a weight depending only on the residual matrix. At the places of q all integral matrices are admitted with the same weight. By the local descriptions of A, B and Λ, the support in A/L lies in the image of A∩Λ. The table is invariant under A∩MΛ, both before and after restricting it to a class modulo the primes g. The translation law shows that α(a)U(a) fixes Θ₀ for a in that subgroup. The modulation law gives the same conclusion for a∈B∩MΛ, since the character of a is trivial on the support by the integrality of the pairing on Λ. These two subgroups sum to MΛ. Indeed, on localising at each finite prime, one of the lattices A and B contains Λ, by the opposite powers of the ideal in halfAO and halfBO. The action twisted by the semicharacter is a genuine action on Λ. Thus α(a)U(a) fixes Θ₀ for every a∈MΛ, independently of the depth of the finite model. The isometry of I and the additive orthogonality at the places of q give the independence before evaluation, also when Φ₀ is a table with complex weights, because its weight is independent of the residues at q.

**Restriction to a torus of dimension fs.** Write Λ₁ for the lattice of one entry, with hermitian form H₁. In the standard pair Λ is the product of sk copies of Λ₁, and H the orthogonal sum of their forms. For y=(y₁,…,yₛ), each y_β with f complex coordinates, put j(y)_{βα}=v_α y_β. For each label l there exists y in the s copies of the scalar lattice B₁ such that j(y) realises the corresponding modulation. Indeed, in row β we extend to its scalar finite group the residue character with coefficients l_{P,β}, trivial on the factors of the other places, and apply perfect duality. Multiplication by the integer v_α produces the required coefficient in each column. The modulation law expresses the labelled section as a non-zero scalar depending on y times exp(−πH(z,jy)) Θ₀(z+jy).

For each derivative ∂ᵘ of the symbol of the arrow, take Fᵤ(y) equal to the value at z=0 of that derivative of the last expression, omitting the scalar. It is a polynomial in the conjugate coordinates of y with coefficients that are entire in the holomorphic sense. The degree in each conjugate variable of row β and embedding τ is at most w_τ, since only the derivatives in that row and embedding can introduce it, and the expansion in minors uses that row at most w_τ times. For every γ∈(MΛ₁)ˢ the period law of Θ₀ gives exactly

    Fᵤ(y+γ) = α(jγ)⁻¹ exp(πH(jy,jγ)+πH(jγ,jγ)/2) Fᵤ(y).

The term πH(z,jγ) of the translation cancels **before** differentiating. Thus all the functions have the same transformation law, whose multiplier is holomorphic in y. The form entering it is D times the sum of s copies of H₁.

**Dimension.** Order lexicographically the monomials allowed in the conjugate variables. The expansion with holomorphic coefficients is unique, by differentiation with respect to those variables. The translation of a monomial produces, besides itself, only others of lower exponent. In the filtration by maximal monomial each quotient injects, by taking the top coefficient, into the holomorphic sections with the same transformation law. This last space has dimension (DM²)^(fs). Indeed, the alternating form of H₁ is unimodular on Λ₁ by self-duality. In a symplectic basis scaled by M, the new form has fs elementary divisors all equal to DM². The dimension formula for theta functions of a positive form is the product of those divisors. It can be seen by Riemann–Roch on the torus: the tangent bundle is trivial, the integral of the top power of the first Chern class divided by (fs)! is that product and the higher cohomology of the positive bundle vanishes. [Schnell, Lecture 5, theta functions of a positive polarisation](https://www.math.stonybrook.edu/~cschnell/mat615/lectures/lecture5.pdf)

There are at most ∏_τ(1+w_τ)ˢ monomials. Each entry of an operator, as a function of the label, is a fixed linear combination of the evaluations of the Fᵤ, up to the same non-zero scalar per label. The rank of the table of entries by labels is bounded by the dimension of the space of those functions. Dividing by N and using qˢ−1≥(q−1)ˢ we obtain the stated inequality. For the all-ones vector, D=k≤b eventually, M≤b¹⁸ by the size of the generating primes, 1+max w_τ≤3b³ and q≥b^(m/2) with m increasing. The base of the power in the bound is thus at most 3b⁴⁰/(q−1).

**How many directions the argument reaches.** If at each place the reduction of v is multiplied by a non-zero scalar, it suffices to absorb it into l_P. For ||v||₂≤q^(1/8) the same rank fraction tends uniformly to zero, since D≤q^(1/4). In the complete system of directions of the source, the fraction of tuples that can be obtained from one of these vectors by such scalars is at most

    (2q^(1/8)+1)^k (q−1)^f /(q^k−1)^f ≤ q^(−kf/2)

for q sufficiently large and k≥8: the numerator is at most q^(k/4+f) and the denominator at least q^((k−1)f). The count by orbits of the previous section allows these directions to be omitted from the source in the chosen bases with that same relative codimension bound. The loss proved in the standard lattices thus reaches a family that is dense in coordinates but sparse by counting. The lower estimate of the rank of the periods on the remaining directions is still pending.

## What is preserved on composing two arrows at an interior vertex

There is a factorisation of the period that separates the theta derivatives from the change of coordinates made between two arrows. It has a consequence for the exceptional directions of the previous section: if a coefficient vector is supported on those directions, on changing coordinates at that same vertex almost all its norm passes to directions of greater height. The estimate holds for any choice of phases of the coefficients.

**One arrow.** Start with a level place. With the other data of an arrow fixed and the level decorations of its endpoints forgotten, let K(X) be its table of operators in the residual matrix X of size s by n. It includes the derivatives and admits complex matrix coefficients. The earlier elimination formula says that the map on functions of covectors is

    (Dφ)(l) = ∑_X K(X) φ(lX),       φ(0)=0.

It also serves for a summand with the other data fixed before summing. Multiplying l by a scalar a transports exactly the same action to the input. Thus the whole route up to the terminal evaluation preserves the characters of scalars separately. In the ramified source only non-trivial characters appear at each place, since the corresponding scalar average is zero. The last pairing with the rows may bring together the images of different characters.

Choose a representative in each line of covectors and one in each line of vectors. Having fixed a multiplicative character χ≠1, an orthonormal basis of its space consists of the functions supported on a line ⟨l⟩ that take the value χ(a)/√(q−1) at al. Extend χ and its inverse by zero at zero. The additive transform with positive sign and normalisation q^(−n/2) takes that space to that of the inverse character on vectors. In the bases by lines its matrix is

    U(w,l) = q^(−n/2) Gχ χ⁻¹(lw),       Gχ = ∑_{a≠0} χ(a) ψ(a).

Indeed, for lw≠0 one changes a to a/(lw) in the sum, and for lw=0 one sums a non-trivial character. U is unitary by the orthogonality of the additive characters and the inverse transformation. Each entry of U in absolute value is zero if lw=0 and q^(−(n−1)/2) otherwise: in each row there are q^(n−1) non-orthogonal lines and all their entries have the same modulus. This also recovers |Gχ|=√q. [Conrad, Gauss and Jacobi sums on finite fields](https://kconrad.math.uconn.edu/blurbs/gradnumthy/Gauss-Jacobi-sums.pdf)

Put K̂(F)=∑_X K(X)ψ(−tr(FX)), unnormalised. The exact matrix of D on the character χ is B U, where for a line of covectors in the target and one of vectors in the source one has

    B(l',w) = q^(−n/2) ∑_{a≠0} χ⁻¹(a) K̂(a w l').

Indeed, one substitutes the inverse transform of φ into φ(l'X). On grouping the vectors of a line the inverse character appears; on reading the output coefficient the two factors √(q−1) of the bases cancel. At several places U is the tensor product of the local U. In B the sum over a and the character are products over the places, but K̂ is the **joint** transform of the table. No independence between its components is required. The value spaces are transported by the identity in U. The formulas are carried out in the class representatives used in the elimination: changing the residual bases transports Fourier by the dual action, so it is compatible with the equivariance under the stabilisers. The class weights and the other summands are preserved. Thus, between two consecutive tables B precisely this unitary change appears, even when the tables have signed weights.

**Transfer between two sets of directions.** At a vertex of dimension n with f places, let A be any set of tuples of lines of covectors and B₀ any set of tuples of lines of vectors. For a vector h supported on A, with coefficients even in an auxiliary hermitian space, one has

    ||1_{B₀} U h||² ≤ [N(A,B₀)/q^((n−1)f)] ||h||²,

where N(A,B₀) counts the pairs of tuples of A and B₀ that have non-zero pairing at all places. It suffices to sum the squares of the entries of the scalar submatrix to bound the square of its operator norm, and then to tensor with the identity. The total norm is preserved. In particular, if the quotient is less than one, projecting Uh to the complement of B₀ remains injective on the whole space supported on A.

Take on both sides the tuples that in the dual bases come from common integer vectors of length at most q^(1/8), as in the previous section. On each side there are at most (2q^(1/8)+1)^n **projective** tuples, because the local rescalings have already disappeared. For q sufficiently large and n≥3, the fraction of squared norm that passes between those two sets is bounded by q^(n/2−(n−1)f). The rest passes to directions that lack a common representative of that length. The bound is uniform in the ramified characters and does not require estimating any theta value.

**Limit of the argument.** For an arbitrary input the above projection does not authorise deleting intermediate directions from the complete composition. A later table B could annihilate the whole complement of B₀. The factorisation identifies the required arithmetic datum more precisely: it is the multiplicative sums of theta coefficients of rank one in B and the relative position of their images and kernels after U. An estimate of the norm of the change of directions alone leaves that datum open.

**The complete composition admits a check directly in the residues.** For a single place, the sum in B is also evaluated in the opposite order. One obtains

    B(l',w) = q^(−n/2) G_{χ⁻¹} ∑_X K(X) χ(−l'Xw).

This holds for matrix tables and at several places with the product of the factors. In the whole chain the same identity can be applied a single time to the initial form: on a path p that starts in a space of dimension k, let v(p) be the tuple of covectors obtained by backward composition from the terminal row. Evaluating at v(p) the inverse transform of a basis vector on the line w gives exactly

    ∏_P [q^(−k/2) G_{χ_P⁻¹}/√(q−1)] · ∏_P χ_P(−v_P(p)w_P).

The paths with some zero pairing contribute zero. This formula uses a single character sum over the complete paths and keeps all the derivatives in its coefficients. On varying the characters there is another way of computing the Gram matrix that eliminates even the Gauss sums.

Indeed, in an initial coarse class, group the path operators of all the rows by the exact value v(p) of the initial argument. Call A(v) the coefficient that multiplies φ(v) in the map to the κ rows. The class weights are included in A. To work with the whole space of functions of the decorations, the map is precomposed with the equivariance average over the stabiliser of the class, as in the earlier framework; this only adds its transports to the coefficients A(v). With the counting product on that space, decompose the non-zero decorations according to the tuple of their lines ℓ and write v=t l_ℓ in fixed representatives, t∈(F_q^×)^f. In each table t↦A(t l_ℓ) subtract successively the mean in each of the f variables and call the result A°_ℓ(t). For the ramified source, the Gram matrix on the rows is exactly

    ∑_{initial classes} ∑_ℓ ∑_t A°_ℓ(t) A°_ℓ(t)*.

Indeed, in the direct sum by lines, subtracting those means is the ramified orthogonal projector. Composing the row operator with the projector is equivalent to applying it to its coefficients in t, because its scalar kernel is real and symmetric. Multiplying by the adjoint gives the formula. The average over the stabiliser commutes with the ramification. The restriction to its image uses the induced product, so that composing beforehand with the average gives the same Gram matrix as that restriction. In particular one obtains the exact arithmetic rank in characteristic zero. For a list of words one sums their Gram matrices on the same rows.

This provides a one-sided certificate for that list: after summing **all** the paths within each argument and subtracting the means, any selection of blocks ℓ may be retained. The rank of the remainder that is retained is a lower bound for that of the complete periods, because each omitted block was positive semidefinite. Thus one only needs to find enough independent rows in some blocks of initial lines. Selecting individual paths within one of those blocks would lose the guarantee. It has not yet been proved how many independent rows the blocks of the hosts of the annex contribute.

## Comparison of ranks at the base tuple alone

For the slope inequality by sampling there is a shortcut in the comparison c5 of the annex. One can dispense with equating the values of the two periods and with fixing a pair of non-zero value per word. We give the precise implication and the lifting identities that justify it. Its realisation in all the arithmetic data is a hypothesis of this section.

Fix a host, its embedding of the field K, the base tuple μ₀ and the identifications of the arithmetic columns with the base periods. Take the same rational basis of the row space R for all words. Write Aᵤ(F) for the functional of the row F on the arithmetic columns and Pᵤ(F') for the spectral functional on the ramified columns, already projected to the good rows. For a list I, its coranks are the dimensions of the intersections of the respective annihilators. It suffices to prove, with a single injective identification J of the rows extended to ℂ, linear or conjugate-linear, that for each word u and each rational row F

    Aᵤ(F)=0 on the whole source  ⇒  Pᵤ(JF)=0 on the whole spectral source.

Indeed, the intersection of the arithmetic kernels of I is defined over K. A basis obtained by elimination over K remains independent after the embedding, and its images under J are independent even if J is conjugate-linear. All of them belong to the spectral intersection. Hence a_ar(I)≤a_esp(I) for **each** list, also with repetitions. Proving the implication only on the vectors of a prefixed basis of R would be insufficient; all their K-linear combinations are needed. This is the reason why the statement on rational rows does serve even though in the end complex ranks are computed.

**Descent of a row that annihilates the columns.** Write m=2d for the length of the chain. For the tuple μ₀ and a word, call col the ordinary composition of its m kernels, and up the composition that inserts the level operator S at each even stage. Let F be a rational row of the annihilator of Aᵤ, represented by its rational lift to the rows before the quotient. We use the following properties of Alpoge's sources at the base tuple:

1. There is a terminal test G for that row, whose reading above gives F and whose descent gives the theta row T_F. The terminal period of col(φ) against G equals Aᵤ(F)(φ) in the indicated coordinates. In the notation of the file these are LinkDown and LinkTail at μ₀. The vanishing extends from rational columns to their complex extension by linearity.
2. For each admissible initial column ψ, the terminal period of up(ψ) against G is a multiple of that of col(ψ') for some initial column of the ramified arithmetic source. This membership in the image after evaluating against G suffices. With the notation of the annex it is obtained from CommX when the iterated level operator preserves that source: the multiple is cS(d) and ψ'=iterS(S₀,ε,d,ψ). ReadS preserves the ramified subspace at each application; one requires in addition to identify it with the source of col, or to verify that the modified columns have a preimage in it. LinkComm at μ₀ also gives this property directly.
3. At the same tuple one has the transport of one term of MainX. Calling P_sup the period of up(ψ) against G and P_inf the lower period against the descended row, the transport identity implies that cTop(d)·P_sup=0 entails cBot·P_inf(y,v)=0 for all y,v. It suffices that cBot≠0, since cBot is the product of the m factors cI(j), all non-zero by the condition on the tuple. LinkZero identifies this lower period with the Bessel period d.per of the column against T_F.

By 1 and 2, the upper period is zero for every column. Taking in MainX an index set with a single element, coefficient one, gives by 3 the vanishing of d.per(φ,T_F)(y,v) for all φ,y,v. No lift of an arbitrary theta vector is involved: only the concrete row is lifted. Nor does one divide by cTop. The transport of one term still contains the descent of the pairs of theta stages. In the source it is obtained by induction from LastX and StepX, applied at the same tuple. Its analytic hypotheses of unfolding and compatibility remain necessary for this deduction.

**From the lower period to the spectral rows.** Assume the lower compatibility BotRel for the adapted datum of the word, with the same central shift z of rows for all words, the spectral evaluation of the descended rows and the dictionary of ramified columns. In BotRel one takes T=T'=T_F and coefficients 1 and 0. Since the lower period is identically zero, every corresponding spectral pair has value zero. The column change R of BotRel is surjective, so that each ramified column is reached; the existence of the evaluation pair and the dictionary are in the first two steps of anchor_pairs. The proof of those two steps uses the evaluation identification and the bijection of columns; the hypothesis PuNZ there is only used afterwards, to produce a pair of non-zero value. The formula PairForm identifies the spectral pairing with a multiple of the value of the pair, with the common conjugation that applies. The central translation is transported to the rows by IsShift, prior to the choice of word. With the injective identification of the good rows this gives exactly Pᵤ(JF)=0. In this application only the rows T_F of the space of lifts are used, for which the evaluation must hold.

This proof of the **implication** locates three savings in the rank step: the tuple μ₀ suffices; the comparison of quotients of constants between tuples and its unit condition at the service primes is omitted; and the production of a non-zero period with which to normalise the others is omitted. The conclusion does not supply those conditions when they are needed elsewhere, for example in the divisors. The points collated are PairForm (BotDat.Rel, BotRel, IsShift), RelOfLinks, ValLastX (StepX, LastX, MainX), TailRd (CommX), ConjugateVectorCentralTwist (anchor_pairs) and JointStarProductNonvanishing (anchor_word), together with the proof of c5 in the annex of 46. The simultaneous existence of the shift and of the row identifications, and the descent laws in the growing hosts, are not deduced from this linear comparison.

**Tolerable exceptions.** Let B be a set of words at which one does not yet know how to transport the annihilator, and rᵤ the rank of the ramified spectral map already projected. On deleting those words from a list I, the spectral corank rises by at most the sum of their rᵤ in the list; the arithmetic corank of the original list is at most that of its good sublist. For h uniform choices among N words there results

    E[a_ar] ≤ E[a_esp] + (h/N) ∑_{u∈B} rᵤ.

Hence it suffices that H h ∑_{u∈B}rᵤ/(Nκ) fits in the slope budget. In particular, the words whose projected spectral map is zero may be omitted in this comparison whatever their number.

## The level operator is transported by an identity of two indicators

At a level place two descriptions of a decoration must be distinguished. Let π be a uniformiser of the local field and q the residue cardinality. For a lattice S, the decorations with lattice S are parametrised by the non-zero covectors f in S/πS; denote the pair by P_f. The decorations **with kernel S** are parametrised by the vectors a≠0 of the same quotient. The pair P⁺_a has lattice S+O·π⁻¹ã and form taking the value zero on S and one at π⁻¹ã, where ã is a lift of a. Changing the lift adds an element of S. Conversely, if a decoration has kernel S, any element of value one determines that unique a on multiplying it by π and reducing. We use Q_l,Q⁺_y for the target lattice L.

The level operator F*, between those two descriptions, has matrix ψ(−f(a)), with ψ a non-trivial additive character. In the product with the inverse Fourier matrix without the zero index there appear q^n δ_{a,b}−1, where n is the rank of the lattice: the character was summed over all covectors except zero. Hence F* has inverse q^(−n) F on the functions of zero sum on each orbit of scalars; the same computation between covectors gives the other composition. The compatibility of the ramification in both descriptions is checked by change of variable in the sum: scaling a by a unit changes the form of the pair P⁺_a in the inverse sense. Scaling a decoration therefore commutes with F* as an operator on pairs. This computation holds with values in any complex space. The formula is natural under isomorphisms of lattices; consequently F and F* preserve the equivariant functions of pairs. The level operator of an arrow includes in addition the rescaling of the decoration by a unit, which is an invertible permutation. The local inversion does not require choosing a non-zero period vector.

**Identity for each matrix.** Let Φ(P,Q;x) be the indicator that x takes the lattice of P to that of Q and that composing x with the form of Q gives that of P. Fix a≠0 and l≠0. Compare the two functions of x

    H₁(x) = ∑_{y≠0} ψ(−l(y)) Φ(P⁺_a,Q⁺_y;x),
    H₂(x) = ∑_{f≠0} ψ(−f(a)) Φ(P_f,Q_l;x).

If xS is not contained in L, both sums are zero. For H₁ this is because x must send the kernel S to the kernel L. In the integral case, let X be the residual matrix S/πS→L/πL. In H₁ there is exactly one candidate y=Xa: the element π⁻¹ã must go to an element of value one. That candidate exists if Xa≠0. In H₂ the only candidate is f=lX and it exists if lX≠0. Their phases coincide when both exist. In short,

    H₁(x) − H₂(x) = 1_{xS⊆L} (1_{lX=0} − 1_{Xa=0}).

Indeed, if one of the two candidates is missing, lXa=0 and the phase is one. For each integral x, the matrix of this difference, with rows l and columns a, has rank at most two and is constant on rescaling either of its indices. On summing against data ramified in a, the difference vanishes **for each x**. The same fact holds on averaging in l and subtracting that average. This proof combines Alpoge's indicator with additive orthogonality. The identity of coefficients, before evaluating the character, was checked in all the 8,928 cases (X,a,l) of the sizes (q,dim S,dim L)=(3,2,2),(2,2,3),(5,1,2).

**Application to the local theta arrow.** Let θ(v,Φ) be the distribution linear in the matrix test and in the input, and K the normalised equivariant arrow of KernelsCommuteTwisted. Write λ_S,λ_L for its two unramified determinant characters and c=λ_S(π)λ_L(π). For an equivariant input h, and base lattices S,L, its two laws of motion are

    Kh(Q_l) = θ(h(P_f),Φ(P_f,Q_l;·))              for all f≠0,
    c Kh(Q⁺_y) = θ(h(P⁺_a),Φ(P⁺_a,Q⁺_y;·))       for all a≠0.

The first identity comes from moving the pairs without changing the lattice: the determinants are units. For the second the two lattices are enlarged in index q. At each end, in an adapted basis, the change has one diagonal factor π⁻¹ and the others are units; the twisted covariance of θ gives the factor written. These are the formulas KK_same and KK_up of the model. Fix l. In the first formula, applied to F*h, take the mean in f and expand F*. In the second take the mean in a and sum in y with weight ψ(−l(y)). Since there is the same number q^(dim S)−1 of f and of a, the difference between c F*(Kh)(Q_l) and K(F*h)(Q_l) is exactly

    (q^(dim S)−1)⁻¹ ∑_{a≠0} θ(h(P⁺_a), H₁−H₂).

Each scalar orbit of a has the same function H₁−H₂ at all its elements. By ramification, the inputs h(P⁺_a) sum to zero on it. The result is K F* = c F* K. Also K commutes with the rescaling of the decorations since Φ is invariant on rescaling both ends simultaneously. This last identity proves in addition that K preserves the ramification. The computation on the base pairs determines the equivariant operator by transport. Thus the local intertwining is obtained directly in the representation used by the arrow, independently of its rank or of the derivatives at the other places. The definitions and the local statement are Alpoge's (HasConductorMulShift, PairOperatorStatement, KernelsCommuteExplicitConst); here the proof uses the explicit defect H₁−H₂.

For a chain of such operators, if K_j S_{j,η}=c_j S_{j+1,η} K_j with the same local label η on both sides and c_j≠0, each insertion S_{2i,ηᵢ} can be passed to the source through 2i arrows. It accumulates the factor ∏_{j<2i}c_j⁻¹. The relative order of the insertions is not altered. On the complete ramified source the product of its operators is invertible by the formula above; for distinct places one acts on distinct factors. Thus the chain with insertions and the ordinary one have the same image under those identifications. For the rank step above the inclusion of images suffices. On applying the conclusion to the global periods it is still necessary to identify the readings of the columns with the local lifts, their combinations and the terminal reading. The laws KBack, ReadS and TailRd of the model fulfil functions distinct from the identity of indicators proved here. Nor has the analytic descent of rows of MainX been obtained by this route.

**Preimage of the modified column at the initial vertex.** The preimage question of point 2 of the previous section admits a formula. In the class model of the construction, let e be the bijection of the initial fine classes with those of the chosen tuple. Its transport E of functions is (Eh)(e c)=h(c). Let e_μ be the identification of the complex space of word columns with the ramified functions of the original source. By definition col₀(φ)=E(e_μ φ). For a modified column ψ of the ramified subspace of the chain, the candidate is, therefore,

    φ = e_μ⁻¹(E⁻¹ψ).

To check that it is defined, it suffices to collate the finite equations of the initial level. In the model of free representatives, the allowed weight is recognised class by class and is preserved under e. For each place v, the operator Nm_v sums the values of the function over the dilations by the residual scalars, with the corresponding frame transport. The bijection e identifies the dilated class and its frame transport for each summand (dil_eCls0 and dilT_eCls0). Hence Nm_v(Eh)(e c)=(Nm_v h)(c). The weight condition and the equations Nm_v=0 are transported in both directions. The local clause ram of PinChL implies that a ψ ramified in the chain satisfies those equations; in the other direction the clause first of Live covers all the initial indices. It follows that E⁻¹ψ is in the domain of e_μ⁻¹ and that col₀(φ)=ψ. This verification does not use a rank estimate nor does it depend on the number of words. It is the specialisation to the source of Alpoge's argument in StepZeroRamifiedFormsTransportIff and TrueGeometryRowColumnEntry. Once the identifications of class and level just made precise are given, the preimage demanded by the descent is not an additional hypothesis. Since it may have complex coefficients, it is applied after extending the rational columns to ℂ, as the argument of the annihilators requires.

## A single archimedean vector for the descent of each pair of stages

In Alpoge's file, ItSpans asserts that certain values I(P)w span the lower module as the polynomial P and the vector w of the upper stage vary. The two infinitesimal actions of the same file give a more precise statement. Write H and H' for the algebraic modules of vectors of finite compact type at the lower and upper stages: at the various archimedean places one takes their algebraic tensor product. For the operators I that satisfy the laws IsItW of the chain and the Fock formulas of both actions, one has

    { I(P)w : P polynomial } = H     for every non-zero w∈H'.

In particular w can be chosen in a minimal compact type and kept while P varies. Here the whole space of polynomials of the operator I is allowed. The statement does not give a maximal degree for the P nor does it say that one and the same P serves for all the vectors of H.

**Proof.** In the discrete series of the model, the Harish-Chandra modules at each place are simple and have scalar endomorphisms for the action of the complex enveloping algebra. This is the property of absolute irreducibility used by Alpoge in KFiniteSpanLeSpanRealIntertwinerValues. The tensor product over the places is also simple for the joint actions. Recall the algebraic reason. In a simple module M with scalar endomorphisms, if x₁,…,xᵣ are linearly independent, the vectors (Ax₁,…,Axᵣ), as A varies in the unital algebra of the action, fill Mʳ: were it a proper submodule of that semisimple module, some non-zero homomorphism Mʳ→M would annihilate it. Such a homomorphism is a scalar combination of the coordinates and taking A=1 would contradict the independence. In a non-zero sum of tensors with independent first components, one can thus keep one component and kill the others. Repeating by induction on the number of factors one obtains a non-zero pure tensor in every non-zero submodule of the product; that tensor generates the product.

The operator I is bilinear in (P,w). Its values on H' lie in H: when the lower compact group acts on I(P)w it suffices to act on P, and the resulting polynomials have bounded degree for fixed P, by the compact Fock formula. Moreover those values are not all zero. Indeed IsItW.ne gives I≠0 on the smooth vectors. The clause bdd bounds each coefficient against a pure tensor of Hilbert vectors by a constant times the product of the input norms. The smooth vectors of finite compact type approximate the smooth ones in norm (the construction by averages further below proves it). By multilinearity and that bound, if all the values on H' were zero, all the coefficients of I on smooth tensors would be zero. The pure tensors of the Hilbert dual separate the algebraic tensors, which would contradict I≠0.

Let X be an element of the lower Lie algebra and Y one of the upper, at any place. There are operators sending polynomials to polynomials, D_X and D'_Y, such that

    X I(P)w = I(D_X P)w,             I(P)(Yw) = −I(D'_Y P)w.

The first formula is eqg and the second invg of IsItW, using respectively the two components of LieFockWith; the scalar trace terms form part of D_X and D'_Y. These equalities extend by linearity to the complexified Lie algebras. The common kernel of all the I(P) on H' is stable under Y, by the second identity. It is proper by the non-vanishing above, hence it is zero by simplicity of H'. Therefore for each w≠0 there is some P with I(P)w≠0. For that fixed w, the image of the linear map P↦I(P)w is a non-zero subspace of H, stable under X by the first identity. The simplicity of H gives the stated equality.

The same w serves after composing two arrows. Fix w≠0 of finite compact type at the upper end and an intermediate vector u≠0 of finite compact type. One chooses P₂ with I₂(P₂)w=u. For any lower vector v of finite compact type there exists P₁ with I₁(P₁)u=v. **One can even fix P₂** for that pair of stages and vary only P₁. This last choice depends on the arrows and on the intermediate vector. It holds likewise with the terminal arrow I_T. By induction, in an arbitrarily long composition of these archimedean operators one can fix the upper vector and all the polynomials except that of the lower arrow. This concerns the operators on archimedean types; the arithmetic kernels and the integrations of the period still intervene.
